# All mass from null edges

**A machine-verified account of where mass comes from, built from a single
primitive: light-speed transport that disagrees with itself.**

Draft v1, 2026-07-08. Status: **[DRAFT-MS]**. This is the *all-mass*
manuscript; it subsumes and cites the P1 origin-of-mass draft
(`Null_Edge_P1_Origin_of_Mass_Manuscript_Draft_v3.md`) rather than
replacing it.

Every technical claim below carries a grade, and the grades are the
point of the paper as much as the claims are:

| Grade | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **T** | source-verified theorem (external mathematics) |
| **M** | machine-verified: kernel-checked in Lean 4 under the pinned toolchain, axiom-audited, guard-pinned |
| **MEMO** | expert-verified prose; kernel transcription pending |
| **C** | pre-registered conjecture with an explicit kill condition |
| **[import]** | an external result used as input, not reproved here |

The discipline this paper holds itself to: **a sentence that claims more
than its grade licenses is an error, however true it may turn out to be.**
Two long-standing conjectures of this program died this month by their own
pre-registered tests (§10). We report those with the same prominence as
the theorems, because a program that cannot say what it has *disproved*
cannot be trusted about what it has proved.

---

## 1. Thesis and reading guide

One sentence: **mass is the obstruction to coherent null transport.**

Unpacked: the only primitive is a *null edge* — an elementary step that
moves at the speed of light, the way a photon does. Nothing in the theory
is slow, and nothing is heavy, at the bottom. Bind several such steps into
one object and ask whether the bundle can still move at light speed. If
its constituent light-directions all agree, it can, and it is massless.
If they disagree, the bundle as a whole cannot keep up with light, and the
amount by which it falls short *is* its mass. Mass is trapped, mutually
disagreeing light.

The organizing slogan of the formal work is **"unification is
decomposition."** A single finite operator — the carrier Dirac operator —
squares to a sum of four terms, and each term is one physical channel
through which mass enters: aperture (kinetic), closure (gauge / QCD), turn
(Higgs / Yukawa), and soldering-gradient (gravity). We do not unify the
forces by identifying them; we unify them by exhibiting them as four
summands of one square (§4).

**How to read this paper.** Part I (§2) is written for a reader who has
seen special relativity and a little quantum mechanics — no gauge theory
assumed. From §3 onward the grades take over and the register is
technical. A reader who wants only the verified core can read §3 (the one
trusted theorem), §4 (the decomposition that organizes everything), and
the anchor table (§11), and skip the rest.

---

## 2. Part I: what is a particle, and what is mass?

*(This section makes no new claims; it is the physical picture behind the
mathematics, in plain language.)*

**A particle is a knot of trapped light.** Picture an electron not as a
tiny ball but as light caught zig-zagging: a left-moving light-step, then
a right-moving light-step, alternating forever. Each leg races at the
speed of light. But a zig-zag that reverses on itself does not *get*
anywhere fast — its average progress is slow, and a particle "at rest" is
the extreme case where the legs cancel and the light runs in place. This
is the old idea that a massive particle is light that has been trapped, and
this program's central theorem (§3) makes it exact: **the mass of a bundle
of light-steps is precisely the total disagreement among their
directions.** All directions parallel: no disagreement, no mass, and the
thing flies off at light speed like a photon. Any disagreement: mass.

**Where mass is made: the corners.** The mass lives not in the legs of the
zig-zag but in the *corners* — the events where the light changes
direction. Take away the ability to turn, and the particle runs straight
at light speed forever, massless. So "how much mass" and "how often it
turns" are the same question. And a turn is not free: turning a
left-handed mover into a right-handed one changes a bookkeeping quantity
(weak charge) that must balance, and the thing that balances it is a field
filling all of space — the Higgs. In this picture the Higgs is not an
optional extra; it is the entry the corner *requires* in order to exist
(§5).

**What a particle is made of: strands.** Internally, a particle's identity
is a short list — which of a few elementary "strands" it carries. Charge
is the bookkeeping of that list: quarks and leptons differ by how many
color strands they hold, which is why quark charges come in thirds; lepton
number and baryon number are just *counts* of strands (the Q04
analysis). An antiparticle is the same list read backwards. The whole
particle zoo of one generation is the catalogue of ways to occupy a
handful of strands.

**Why particles are stable: topology.** Some patterns cannot come apart,
not because a force holds them but because a *count* forbids it. When the
left-handed and right-handed slots fail to balance, the surplus cannot
find a partner to turn with, and so it cannot acquire mass no matter what
fields you switch on. It stays massless the way a knot stays knotted (§8).
This is the program's reading of why masslessness in the Standard Model
tracks chirality.

**Nothing moves slower than light — fundamentally.** Every edge is null.
The electron on your desk is, in this ontology, moving at light speed the
entire time — it simply is not *going* anywhere, because its light-steps
disagree and cancel. "Slower than light" is what the statistics of
disagreeing light-steps looks like from far away. There is no slow
substance underneath.

That is the entire picture. The rest of the paper is the mathematics that
makes each italicized claim precise, and honest about which are theorems
and which are still hopes.

---

## 3. The kinematic layer: one trusted theorem (**T**/**M**)

Everything orbits a single kernel-checked identity. Represent a massless
degree of freedom by a two-component Weyl spinor `psi`; its contribution
to energy-momentum is the rank-one Hermitian matrix `psi psi^dagger`. A
particle-like system is a finite bundle of these, with total momentum
`P = sum_i psi_i psi_i^dagger`. Then

```text
det P = sum_{i<j} | psi_i wedge psi_j |^2 .
```

The left side is invariant mass squared. The right side is the total
pairwise *disagreement* of the null directions — the sum of squared
wedges, which vanishes exactly when two directions are parallel. So:

- A single null edge is massless: `det (psi psi^dagger) = 0`
  (`det_rankOneHermitian_eq_zero`, **M**, trusted namespace
  `PhysicsSM.Spinor.PluckerMass`); and the two-edge mass identity and its
  collinearity criterion are trusted there too (`two_edge_plucker_mass_identity`,
  `two_edge_mass_zero_iff_wedge_zero`).
- Mass equals total pairwise disagreement for any finite bundle
  (`fin_bundle_plucker_mass_identity`, **M**; the general `n`-bundle
  version is kernel-checked in the Draft namespace).
- Mass is exactly zero iff all directions are projectively collinear —
  one common beam (`fin_bundle_mass_zero_iff_common_direction`, **M**, Draft).

This is the precise form of "mass is trapped disagreeing light," and it is
the strongest thing the program owns: kernel-checked, axiom-audited, in the
trusted layer. It is also *only* kinematics — it says what mass *is* for a
given bundle, not what dynamics builds the bundle. The rest of the paper is
about the dynamics, and it is held to a lower grade for exactly that
reason.

---

## 4. The organizing spine: the mass-budget decomposition (**M** + **C**)

The dynamical object is the finite carrier Dirac operator on a finite
2-complex,

```text
D = sum_e c(alpha_e) nabla_e + Gamma phi ,
```

with a null covector soldering `c(alpha_e)` on each edge (a Clifford
coefficient, `c(alpha)^2 = 0`), a covariant transport `nabla_e`, and a
vertex "turn" term `Gamma phi`. The master identity of the whole program is
that its Krein-adjoint square decomposes into channels:

```text
4 . D^#D  =  Q_A  +  Q_C  +  4 Q_T  +  E_#            (carrier_krein_square, M)
```

and every other channel statement is a specialization of this one equation.
Each summand is one physical channel, and the reader can carry this table
through §§5–9:

| channel | operator shape | force | how the invariant enters | positivity |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `Q_A` | aperture / `{nabla, nabla}` | kinetic | Plücker mass of §3 (`det P`) | positive (§3) |
| `Q_C` | closure / `[gamma,gamma][nabla,nabla]` | gauge / QCD | chromomagnetic `sigma·F`, §6 | signed (§6/§8) |
| `4 Q_T` | turn / `phi^2` | Higgs / Yukawa | corner amplitude, §5 | turn-sign |
| `E_#` | Krein self-adjointness defect | — | cross term, §7 | vanishes in the self-adjoint gauge class |

**Two specializations, both kernel-checked.** In the self-adjoint gauge
class the cross term `E_#` vanishes, and the master identity reduces to the
three-slot square `4 D^2 = Q_A + Q_C + 4 Q_T` (`carrier_square_assembly`,
**M**) — this is the form §§5–6 use. Separately, for *varying* soldering the
gravity channel is a genuinely distinct object, the soldering-gradient
defect `E` of `weitzenbock_master_varying` (**M**, §7) — note this `E` (a
`D^2`-defect measuring non-constancy of the soldering) and the Krein
cross-term `E_#` above are two different blocks; identifying them is a
conjecture (**C**), not a theorem.

**Unification is decomposition.** These are not four theories glued
together; they are four summands of one square. The claim the program
stakes is that *the* invariant — pairwise null disagreement — reappears in
each channel through a different canonical map.

**The budget corollary (M).** A one-line consequence of the assembly
(apply any linear expectation `ev` — the state functional `<psi, . psi>` —
and divide by `M^2 = 4 ev(D^2) != 0`): the channel shares

```text
b_A + b_C + b_T = 1 ,
```

is kernel-checked (`signed_budget_sum_one`, **M**), with a concrete
non-vacuous witness: a single-edge `2x2` carrier gives closure share
exactly zero (one edge, no closure) and budget `(1/2, 0, 1/2)`
(`witness_budget_sum_one`, **M**). Two honesty rails, both load-bearing:

1. **The shares are signed.** We do *not* call them positive fractions.
   Whether a channel share is positive is the closure-positivity question
   of §6 — now *resolved*: `b_C` is genuinely signed (the closure form is
   balanced on the physical sector), so `b_C` can be negative on some
   states. This is not a defect: §8 explains why the physics of chiral
   symmetry breaking *requires* the closure channel to have negative
   directions.
2. **`b_C` is the chromomagnetic share, not "gluon energy."** The closure
   *channel* `Q_C` is linear in field strength (a `sigma·F` /
   chromomagnetic object); the `|F|^2` gluon *energy* density is a
   different object (the Wilson action, §6). Conflating them is a
   pre-registered error (§10).

The physical target this shape is aimed at — a finite analogue of the Ji
decomposition of the proton mass — is grade **C**: the weak claim
(non-turn dominance, `|b_T|` small, matching that ~99% of nucleon mass is
not Higgs-generated `[import]`: Yang et al., proton mass decomposition) is
the honest first goal; the strong claim (closure is the single largest
share) is a separate, harder, falsifiable test.

## 5. Turn mass: the Higgs-shaped channel (**M** + a reported kill)

The turn block `Q_T = phi^2` is where mass enters at a corner. The
mechanism, in the program's language: a corner converts a left-handed
light-mover into a right-handed one; the two carry different weak charge;
the corner must therefore exchange weak charge with a background
condensate; that condensate is the Higgs. The corner amplitude *is* the
mass. This is a **MEMO**-grade reading, resting on the kinematic corner
identity (`onshell_wedge_normSq_eq_coin_sq`, **M**, kernel-checked in
`GateI1/MassCoinBridge.lean` — a supporting identity, not guard-pinned;
§11) and the Standard-Model strand bookkeeping (Q04, **MEMO**).

**A reported kill (this is the honest heart of the section).** The program
attempted to derive the *value* of the charged-lepton mass ratios — the
Koide relation `Q = 2/3` — from corner geometry, via a soldering
coefficient `kappa` that would have to equal 1. A pre-registered numerical
probe measured it: `kappa = 3/2`, not 1, predicting `Q = 5/9` against the
observed `2/3`, and the carrier reduction does not even produce the
required uniform-diagonal form. **The tetrahedral-corner Koide route is
dead** (probe P1, `TSOLDER_KAPPA_ANALYSIS.md` §4a). What survives is the
equipartition trace identity behind the Koide *combination* (pure algebra,
unaffected) and a sharper open question — see §8. Any future mass-value
route must additionally clear the Sumino bar `[import]`: a real Koide
mechanism must survive QED running, which this route never reached. The
honest status of mass *values* in this program is therefore: **no live
prediction**; ratios, not absolute scales, are the only admissible targets
(§10).

---

## 6. Closure mass: the QCD-shaped channel (**M** + the central crux, resolved)

Most visible mass is QCD binding energy. In this program it lives in the
closure channel, and the closure channel is where the program is,
surprisingly, furthest along outside pure kinematics.

**The Wilson action is a squared closure defect (M).** Before any carrier
identification, the standard lattice gauge action is *exactly* the squared
norm of the failure of transport to close around a face: for a face
holonomy `U`,

```text
Tr((1 - U)^dag (1 - U)) = 2N - 2 Re Tr U ,
```

so the Wilson plaquette weight `N - Re Tr U` is half the Hilbert-Schmidt
square of the closure defect `1 - U`
(`wilson_plaquette_eq_half_closure_defect`, **M**;
`closure_defect_trace_eq`, **M**). QCD's action and the program's closure
channel are the same object at the source. And this squared defect *is
positive energy*: for the linearized connection its leading value is the
non-negative Hilbert-Schmidt norm `-Tr(A²) = ‖A‖² = |F|²` at leading order
(`leading_closure_energy_nonneg`, **M**), zero exactly at flatness. The
static-pair potential then reads as the transfer-time cost of excess areal
closure defect (**C**, Amendment A2). (This `|F|²` *defect-gram* energy is
distinct from the chromomagnetic `Q_C` channel — §4 rail 2.)

**The strong-coupling pillars are kernel-checked (M).** On concrete finite
lattices: the Wilson-loop area law (`tyAreaLaw_slab_exp`), slab reflection
positivity (`wilsonSlabConnected_reflectionPositive`), an OS-reconstructed
spectral gap (`osSpectralGap_pos`), and exponential clustering
(`slab_exponential_clustering`) — the two hard pillars of confinement and
mass gap, in their strong-coupling forms. The one remaining hole in the
gap chain is a finite forest-counting injection, now diagnosed (this
month, audit memo, **MEMO**) as a *malposed statement* rather than a hard
proof: the total-block
permutation count collapses under the root-pinning constraint, so the
structured-partition route is the only viable one
(`PolymerKPConclusion.lean`; K1-STEP0 audit).

**The closure channel is an exact Krein square — and this relocates the
crux (M + MEMO).** The nonabelian closure block factors exactly:

```text
Q_C = L^# L ,   L = c(alpha_1) (x) 1 + c(alpha_2) (x) (-K/2),
      K = [nabla_1, nabla_2] ,
```

with the abstract square identity kernel-checked (`null_soldered_square`,
`closure_current_square`, **M** — a group-free ring identity with explicit
hypotheses, which is *stronger* in that direction); the group-independence
(any compact group) and the GL-torsor classification of representatives are
**MEMO**, oracle-verified across SU(2) and SU(3)). But a Krein square carries no
positivity by itself — null Clifford coefficients are isotropic, so the
square has no positive-definite diagonal. Therefore:

> **The central crux, resolved as a structured no-go (M engine + MEMO).**
> Positivity of the closure channel is not a full-space fact and never
> could be; it can hold only on the physical (Gauss-law) sector `V'/N`.
> The resolution (Fable analysis, this run): closure is **not** positive
> there — it is exactly *balanced* (Krein signature zero), structurally.
> The mechanism is a grading anticonjugation: the closure bivector
> `b = sigma_z (x) 1` satisfies `b^{-1}(J Q_C) b = -(J Q_C)` and preserves
> every gauge-defined constraint sector (gauge acts on the color factor
> alone, commuting with `b`), and a Hermitian form congruent to its own
> negative has equal positive and negative inertia. The kernel engine is
> proved: anticonjugation forces every odd power traceless
> (`anticonj_odd_pow_trace_zero`, **M**), so the form's spectrum is
> symmetric about zero; the half-constraint rigidity that forces the
> single-covector Gauss charge is also kernel-checked
> (`half_constraint_rigidity`, **M**); and the balanced inertia is
> confirmed on the `6x6` witness by the pre-registered numeric probe
> (`sig = (2,2,0)`, oracle). So `Q_C` is honestly a *signed* chromomagnetic
> channel; physical positivity must come from the `J`-definite complement
> of the closure doublet (the matter/transverse directions), exactly as in
> Gupta–Bleuler the longitudinal pair contributes zero norm. Two clauses
> stay MEMO pending their own rungs: the concrete `V'` construction from
> the carrier Gauss covectors, and the step from odd-moment-vanishing to
> the inertia count.

A second correction the resolution forces: the gate as originally posed
asked whether a torsor *representative* `L_A` descends to `V'`; it does
not (**MEMO**), but that was the wrong question — only the *square* `Q_C`
needs to descend, and it does iff the finite Ward condition
`K(ker G) subseteq ker G` holds. The existing finite Kugo–Ojima witnesses
frame the surviving question: the nonvacuous positive sector on `(2,1)`
(`nonvacuous_positive_sector`, **M**) and the indefinite no-go on `(1,2)`
(`nondegenerate_but_indefinite_no_go`, **M**) show the decision quantity is
the inertia surplus `p - q` on the doublet-free complement — now with a
mechanism. Scope, stated plainly: everything here is finite and
strong-coupling; the continuum Yang–Mills mass gap is the Clay problem and
is **not claimed** (§10).

## 7. Soldering mass: the gravity-shaped channel (**M** + **C**)

The soldering-gradient block `E` is the gravity-shaped channel: it
measures how the null soldering fails to be covariantly constant, via the
frame commutator `D(e,f) = nabla_e gamma_f - gamma_f nabla_e`. The finite
"geometric trinity" split is now kernel-checked:

```text
2 E = Contract(T) + Contract(S)
```

(`eslot_torsion_solder_split`, **M**, choice-free), with `T` the
antisymmetrized soldering difference (torsion-shaped) and `S` a symmetric
remainder (non-metricity-shaped). And the split is *non-trivial*: the
program's earlier conjecture that `E` is *pure* torsion is refuted by an
explicit `2x2` witness where the symmetric contraction does not vanish
(`eslot_not_pure_torsion_witness`, **M**; §10). So at finite algebraic
level the gravity channel is a torsion-plus-non-metricity mix, not pure
teleparallel — the corrected statement after the pure-torsion kill.

What remains conjectural (**C**) is the *geometric* reading: identifying
`T` and `S` with a discrete contorsion and non-metricity carrying the
right transformation law, with the discrete teleparallel /
symmetric-teleparallel literature `[import]` (Pereira–Vargas and
Regge-adjacent work) as the anchor. This is still the least-developed
channel physically, and the honest content is mostly the boundary, per the
Malament split: causal order supplies the light-cone structure for free,
and the decorations owe exactly the scale. But the finite *algebra* of the
split is a theorem, not a hope.

---

## 8. Protected masslessness: topology forbids mass (**M**)

Some modes cannot acquire mass, and this is a theorem, not a tuning. The
finite McKean–Singer index family shows that for a rank-symmetric carrier
the chiral index equals the graded dimension
(`chiralIndex_eq_graded_dimension`, **M**), and an unbalanced count forces
an exact massless mode immune to every potential and transport
(`exists_protected_massless_mode`, **M**). Masslessness of the chiral
surplus is topological — the knot of Part I, made precise.

**A new protection mechanism, found this month (M + C).** A determinant-
parity probe redirected a stalled line of work: the protected zero modes of
the decorated transport cycle are *not* forced by cyclic symmetry (that
reading was falsified — abstract symmetric data is generically unpinned),
but by a **chiral** symmetry — an involution `Gamma` with
`Gamma W Gamma = W^dagger`, which is exactly the edge-orientation-reversal
grading that also gives the program's Ginsparg–Wilson structure. Its
kernel-checked core: a unitary carrying such an involution has determinant
`+-1` (`chiral_det_eq_pm_one`, **M**); by the standard conjugate-pairing of
unitary spectra (**T**, transcription pending) that sign pins the parity of
the `-1`-eigenvalue multiplicity (the Lean file states the determinant fact;
the multiplicity reading is prose, per its own docstring). The full
amplitude-independent *double* pinning (both `±1` at every hop strength) is
**not** a global winding invariant — that index was measured to vanish
here. It is an equivariant *reflection-sectored* index: `W` commutes with a
reflection `R` (leg-reversal ∘ orientation-swap), and the two `R`-sectors
carry opposite chiral indices that cancel globally but each pin one `±1`
mode. The sector index is a Lefschetz fixed-point count `±¼ Tr(ΓR)` that
does not involve `W` — hence the `|t|`-independence (grade **C** /
**M**-target, with a rational fixture and a spectral-theorem-free pinning
theorem in hand; Fable analysis this run). The resulting spectrum on the
small cycle is neutrino-shaped (one exactly massless mode; oracle, **C**) —
which is where the mass-value question, having failed for charged leptons
(§5), honestly relocates.

**Why indefiniteness is a feature, not a bug.** The closure channel's
global indefiniteness (§6) is *required* here: chiral symmetry breaking
needs the curvature term to pull eigenvalues toward zero against the
positive kinetic part, so a positive-definite closure channel would have
killed this mechanism outright. §6 and §8 are coupled in the right
direction: the same sign structure that blocks naive closure positivity is
what makes protected and near-protected light modes possible.

---

## 9. Dynamics: mass generation under coarse-graining (**M**)

The kinematic theorem (§3) says what mass *is*; this section's theorem says
coarse-graining *makes* it, from the same invariant. One decimation
(Schur-complement) step on a null chain — integrating out a hidden site —
converts square-zero (null) edge terms into a non-null effective term. The
abstract law is

```text
(a b)^2 = k . (a b)   for   a^2 = b^2 = 0,  a b + b a = k . 1
```

(`null_pair_prod_sq_eq_pairing_smul`, **M**): the effective term fails to
be nilpotent exactly when the pairing `k` is nonzero — and for null
directions, nonzero pairing means non-collinear, i.e. *disagreeing*. On the
concrete three-site chain the induced edge is a nonzero idempotent
(`effective_edge_not_nilpotent`, **M**), where none existed before
blocking. The negative control is what makes this a statement about mass:
collinear couplings produce exactly zero effective coupling
(`collinear_schurComplement_eq_zero`, **M**) — nullity survives blocking
precisely on the massless configurations.

So the program's thesis is two-sided, and both sides are kernel-checked:
*kinematically* mass is pairwise null disagreement (§3); *dynamically*,
coarse-graining converts that same disagreement into an effective mass
term, and converts nothing when there is no disagreement.

**The coupling is a propagator element (M).** For a general (non-scalar)
invertible hidden block, the effective edge term is
`c(l) Minv c(n) = (Minv)_{11} . (c(l) c(n))`
(`nullL_mul_mid_mul_nullN`, **M**): the generated coupling is exactly the
matrix element of the hidden-block resolvent between the two null
light-cone directions, and it is non-nilpotent iff that element is nonzero
(`mid_effective_not_nilpotent`, **M**). This is the expected physics — the
effective coupling between two null directions is their propagator
overlap — and it recovers the scalar case as `Minv = mu⁻¹ . 1`.

Claim boundary: one finite decimation step — no renormalization-group
flow, no fixed point, no continuum. The bridge from this step to
constituent-mass generation was conjectured (Amendment A4) as "blocking a
closure-disordered background increases the finite near-zero count `N_m`
of §6". A pre-registered probe this run **refutes that at the finite
random-disorder level**: both generic and chiral-preserving random
closure disorder *decrease* `N_m` (they spread the spectrum away from
zero). So the Banks–Casher accumulation that would signal a condensate is
*not* produced by finite random curvature; it needs a specific coherent /
topological low-mode structure or a thermodynamic limit. The §9→§6 bridge
is therefore a documented kill at this level, and the honest open question
is sharper: *which* structured (not random) closure backgrounds accumulate
low modes. Grade **C**, with the naive version now closed.

---

## 10. Boundaries, and the things we have disproved

**The permanent boundary.** No continuum limit is claimed. No physical mass
scale (dimensional transmutation) is claimed. Nothing Clay-adjacent is
claimed. The only sanctioned limit language is the refinement-ladder
(quotient-then-limit) discipline, and the only admissible mass targets are
dimensionless ratios protected by finite structure — never absolute MeV
values (finding 9; NuFIT-6.0 `[import]` for the one neutrino ratio that is
a legitimate finite target).

**The kills — reported with the prominence of the theorems.** A reader
familiar with the field will expect several natural ideas; here is why each
is dead, so no one re-derives them:

- **Koide from tetrahedral corner geometry** — killed by measurement
  (`kappa = 3/2`, probe P1). The equipartition identity survives as algebra
  (§5).
- **"`Tr E` = discrete torsion"** — killed by probe; replaced by the
  trinity-split target (§7).
- **"`Q_C` = site-diagonal defect Gram"** — killed structurally (grading:
  `Q_C` is purely off-diagonal, orthogonal to every site-local Gram). The
  defect Gram is a real object — it is the Wilson action — just not this
  operator (§6).
- **"`Q_C` is the positive gluon-energy share"** — killed by the
  chromomagnetic distinction: `Q_C` is linear in `F` (hyperfine-shaped);
  the `|F|^2` energy is the defect Gram (§4, §6).
- **Cyclic symmetry forces the protected zero mode** — falsified; the
  correct mechanism is chiral, not cyclic (§8).
- **Retardedness alone deletes fermion doublers** — killed by a
  determinant-level obstruction; one-sided Ginsparg–Wilson inversion is
  false nonabelian (explicit counterexample); the palindromic transfer
  ordering is the correct convention.
- **Spectral-measure language before positivity** — embargoed
  program-wide; finite eigenvalue-*count* identities are the sanctioned
  form (§6's Banks–Casher count, `banks_casher_count`, **M**).
- **"Random closure disorder increases the near-zero count `N_m`"** (the
  naive §9→§6 constituent-mass bridge, Amendment A4) — killed by a
  pre-registered probe: finite random curvature, chiral or generic,
  *decreases* `N_m` by spreading the spectrum. Condensate accumulation
  needs structured, not random, low-mode content (§9).

**The open cruxes, ranked** (after this run's progress). The former #1 —
physical-sector closure positivity (S1-CC) — is now *resolved* as a
structured no-go (§6): closure is balanced, its algebraic engine is
kernel-checked, and the pre-registered numeric kill probe passed. What
remains: (1) the surviving positivity question it exposed — total-operator
positivity on the doublet-free complement, i.e. an aperture/turn-dominance
inequality over the `J`-definite directions (§6). (2) The strong-coupling
gap's forest injection (§6) — now a well-posed combinatorics problem, the
malposed-statement diagnosis in hand. (3) The multi-direction closure
square (a stabilization, largely dissolved). (4) The chiral winding
invariant for the double-pinning (§8). (5) Completing the S1-CC ladder in
Lean (Theorems 1–3: descent, restricted inertia, the grading no-go —
oracle-confirmed, transcription pending). Each is finite, each has a kill
condition, none requires new axioms.

/-
# Aperture dominance => total-operator positivity on the physical sector

DRAFT (kernel-clean; no `s o r r y`). The opener for the program's #1
next target after the S1-CC resolution (Fable call-02, Part C): total
-operator positivity on the doublet-free complement. This is the
program-wide bottleneck - the spectral-language rail lifts only when it
resolves, it is the precondition for the first-meson (S5) statement, and
it gives the finite Banks-Casher count its physical reading.

## The statement

S1-CC showed the closure channel is BALANCED (a bounded, SIGNED
perturbation) on the physical sector. The question is then whether the
aperture (kinetic) channel - which IS positive, its dominance constant the
trusted-core pairwise-disagreement gap - dominates the signed closure
channel on the doublet-free complement. This module lands the abstract
dominance rung:

`aperture_dominance_pos`: if a Hermitian-form split `A + C` has the
aperture part `A` bounded below by `c > 0` on a subspace `P`, and the
balanced part `C` bounded in absolute value by `kappa < c` there, then
`A + C` is positive-definite on `P`.

The physics then lives in two follow-up rungs (Fable Part C, handoff):
(rung 2) `kappa <= ||bivector|| * ||K||` for the closure channel by
submultiplicativity; (rung 3) compute `c` and `kappa` on the 6x6 S1-CC
witness by rational arithmetic - the first kernel-checked total-operator
positivity witness on a physical sector. The general Weyl eigenvalue-shift
refinement is an Aristotle package (Weyl inequalities are likely a genuine
Mathlib gap).

## Claim boundary

This is the pure inequality: bounded-below-plus-bounded-signed is positive
when the gap wins. No physics is claimed here; `A`, `C`, `c`, `kappa`, `P`
are abstract. Instantiating them with the carrier aperture/closure channels
and the physical sector is the (handed-off) content. No positivity of the
closure channel itself is claimed - the point is precisely that closure is
signed and only the SUM is positive.

## Provenance

Fable call-02 (2026-07-08), Part C #1 - the recommended house-style opener
- [orig]/[interp]; the dominance inequality is elementary - [import].
-/

import Mathlib

namespace PhysicsSM.Draft.NullEdge.Carrier.ApertureDominancePositivity

open Matrix

variable {n : Type*} [Fintype n]

/-- **Aperture dominance rung.** For matrices `A` (aperture, positive) and
`C` (balanced closure, signed) and a subspace `P`, if the aperture
quadratic form is bounded below by `c > 0` on `P` and the closure form is
bounded in magnitude by `kappa < c` on `P`, then `A + C` is
positive-definite on `P \ {0}`. The gap `c - kappa` is the positivity
budget; in the physical instantiation `c` is the aperture (pairwise
-disagreement) gap and `kappa` the balanced-closure bound. -/
theorem aperture_dominance_pos (A C : Matrix n n ℂ)
    (P : Submodule ℂ (n → ℂ)) (c κ : ℝ) (hκc : κ < c)
    (hA : ∀ v ∈ P, c * ‖v‖ ^ 2 ≤ (star v ⬝ᵥ A.mulVec v).re)
    (hC : ∀ v ∈ P, |(star v ⬝ᵥ C.mulVec v).re| ≤ κ * ‖v‖ ^ 2) :
    ∀ v ∈ P, v ≠ 0 → 0 < (star v ⬝ᵥ (A + C).mulVec v).re := by
  intro v hv hv0
  have hsplit : (star v ⬝ᵥ (A + C).mulVec v).re
      = (star v ⬝ᵥ A.mulVec v).re + (star v ⬝ᵥ C.mulVec v).re := by
    rw [Matrix.add_mulVec, dotProduct_add, Complex.add_re]
  have hn : 0 < ‖v‖ ^ 2 := by
    have : 0 < ‖v‖ := norm_pos_iff.mpr hv0
    positivity
  have hAbound := hA v hv
  have hCbound := (abs_le.mp (hC v hv)).1
  have key : 0 < c * ‖v‖ ^ 2 - κ * ‖v‖ ^ 2 := by nlinarith [hn, hκc]
  rw [hsplit]
  linarith [hAbound, hCbound, key]

/-! ## Local axiom guard (self-contained) -/

/-- info: 'PhysicsSM.Draft.NullEdge.Carrier.ApertureDominancePositivity.aperture_dominance_pos' depends on axioms: [propext, Classical.choice, Quot.sound] -/
#guard_msgs (whitespace := lax) in
#print axioms aperture_dominance_pos

end PhysicsSM.Draft.NullEdge.Carrier.ApertureDominancePositivity

```

# Fable-5 call 03 — response

## Alignment flags (required source check, before the parts)

I based everything below on the verbatim artifacts and the repo files (`S1CCBalancedInertia.lean`, `CarrierIndexProtection.lean`, `ChiralZeroModeParity.lean`, `CarrierMassBudget.lean`, `CarrierSquareAssembly.lean`, `CarrierApertureIdentification.lean`, read this call). 2. **`witness_budget_sum_one` vs manuscript §4**: the kernel pins *sum = 1* and *Q_C = 0* (`witness_QC_zero`); the individual values `(1/2, 0, 1/2)` are derivable from `hDD` but are **not separately stated theorems**. The manuscript's "budget `(1/2, 0, 1/2)` (`witness_budget_sum_one`, M)" is a hair of docstring-outruns-kernel. Fix: either add `witness_bA_half` or soften to "with closure share exactly 0 (kernel-pinned) and shares `(1/2,0,1/2)` (arithmetic consequence)".
3. **`aperture_dominance_pos`**: sound as stated; two instantiation traps. (a) The conclusion is positivity of `Re⟨v,(A+C)v⟩`; for non-Hermitian `A+C` that is weaker than operator positivity — the physical instantiation must feed the **Krein form**, i.e. `J·(Q_A+Q_C)`, not the bare blocks. (b) `c > 0` is not hypothesized; it is implied (`hC` forces `κ ≥ 0` on any nonzero `v ∈ P`, and `κ < c`). Harmless, but the docstring says "`c > 0`" — note the discrepancy.
4. **`ChiralInvolution`** still omits `Γᴴ = Γ`/unitarity (known, harmless for the det theorems; the sector-index integrality in Part A below *will* want unitary `Γ`, so add it in the new structure, not retroactively).
5. §3/§6/§8 manuscript rows checked against the files read: aligned; the `closure_current_square` MEMO demotion for the concrete `Q_C = L^#L` is correctly worded.

---

## Part A — the unifier: one finite equivariant index theorem (partial unification: 3 of 4, plus a bridge)

**Answer up front:** there is a single finite framework of which McKean–Singer protection, the C4 sectored pinning, and the S1-CC balanced inertia are *literal corollaries*. The RG-Schur dichotomy is **not** an instance — it is a different logical genus — but it attaches through a bridge theorem (index invariance under decimation). Claiming 4/4 would be false shape; 3/4 + bridge is the honest organizing theorem.

### The master theorem (MEMO now; each clause an M-target)

**Data.** A finite-dimensional ℂ-space `V` (the decorated complex's state space); a symmetry group `Ĝ ⊆ GL(V)` with a **parity character** `σ : Ĝ → {±1}`; a dynamical operator in one of two faces, equivariant *up to parity*:

- **additive face**: `X : V → V` with `g X g⁻¹ = X` for even `g`, `g X g⁻¹ = −X` for odd `g`;
- **multiplicative face**: unitary `W` with `g W g⁻¹ = W` for even `g`, `g W g⁻¹ = W† (= W⁻¹)` for odd `g`;

and any subspace `P ⊆ V` (constraint/physical sector) invariant under `X` (resp. `W`) and under the even subgroup, decomposed into isotypic sectors `V_χ`.

**Conclusions.**

- **(i) Balance (odd element ⇒ Witten index zero).** If an odd `s ∈ Ĝ` preserves `P`: `charpoly(X|_P)` is negation-symmetric (additive), hence for Hermitian `X`, `n₊ = n₋` on `P`; multiplicatively, `spec(W|_P)` is inversion-symmetric and `det W|_P = ±1`, pinning the parity of `mult(−1)`.
- **(ii) Index (even element vs an odd involution).** If `Γ ∈ Ĝ` is an odd involution (`ΓXΓ = −X`), then for every even `g` commuting with `Γ`, the **Lefschetz supertrace** `L(g) := tr((Γ·g)|_P)` localizes to the kernel: `L(g) = tr((Γ·g)|_{ker X ∩ P})`. In particular `g = 1` gives `tr Γ|_P = ind(X|_P)` (= graded dimension), and `dim(ker X ∩ P) ≥ |L(1)|`.
- **(iii) Sector pinning (multiplicative face).** If `Γ` is an odd involution (`ΓWΓ = W†`) and `R` an even involution commuting with both, then `C := ΓW` is an involution, and in each `R`-sector `V_χ` the integers `ν₀(χ) = ½ tr((Γ + ΓW)P_χ)`, `ν_π(χ) = ½ tr((Γ − ΓW)P_χ)` satisfy `dim(ker(W∓1) ∩ V_χ) ≥ |ν(χ)|`; when `tr(ΓW P_χ)` collapses (half-winding case) the value is the fixed-point count `±¼ tr(ΓR)` — a Lefschetz number not involving `W`, whence amplitude-independence.

**The unifying observation, stated plainly:** the program discovered three gradings separately — the chirality `Γ`, the closure bivector `b = σ_z ⊗ 1`, and the edge-orientation-reversal `Γ` of the GW structure — and they are all **odd elements of one group `Ĝ` acting on the decorated complex**, while gauge transformations and the reflection `R` are even elements. The master invariant is one object, the equivariant supertrace `tr(Γ g |sector)`; its three uses are `g = 1` (McKean–Singer), `g = R` (C4), and "an odd element exists" (S1-CC = the closure form has an exact supersymmetry, so its Witten index vanishes sector-by-sector). It is visible in the code that `b` and the witness grading `GammaW` are *the same matrix* `σ_z` — that is not a coincidence; it is the framework.

### Corollary specializations

| Landed result | Master clause | Specialization |
|---|---|---|
| `chiralIndex_eq_graded_dimension` (M) | (ii), `g = 1` | `Ĝ = ⟨Γ⟩`; note the Lean file is *more general* (rank hypothesis over any `DivisionRing`); the master recovers its `chiralIndex_adjoint_pair` / `chiralIndex_krein_pair` faces |
| C4 sectored pinning (`dim ker(W∓1)∩V_χ ≥ |ν(χ)|`) (M-target, specced) | (iii) | `Ĝ = ⟨Γ, R⟩`, `ν = ±¼ tr(ΓR)` = fixed-leg count |
| S1-CC balanced inertia (`anticonj_charpoly_eq` + capstone) | (i), additive | odd `s = b = σ_z⊗1`; sectors = Gauss sectors, invariant because the (even) gauge group commutes with `b` |
| `chiral_det_eq_pm_one` (M) | (i), multiplicative, determinant shadow | already landed as the `det` face |
| **RG-Schur dichotomy** | **not an instance** | see below |

**Why RG-Schur does not fit (be precise about the partial unification):** `null_pair_prod_sq_eq_pairing_smul` is a *ring identity about products* — `(ab)² = k·(ab)` — quantitative in the pairing `k`. No trace/homotopy invariant reproduces "the effective edge is a nonzero idempotent": index-type invariants are deformation-invariant integers; the RG-Schur output is a continuous coefficient (the propagator element `(M⁻¹)₁₁`). The two attach at exactly one theorem, which I recommend naming as the bridge:

> **Bridge (C / M-target): `schur_complement_preserves_sector_index`.** If `V = V_keep ⊕ V_hide` with the splitting `Ĝ`-invariant and `Γ`-invariant, `X` odd, and the hidden block invertible, then the Schur complement is odd for the restricted grading and has the same sector indices `L(g)`. Physics: **the index says what decimation cannot gap; the pairing law says what it does gap.** Hypotheses (grading-balance of `V_hide`, invertibility uniformly in the even action) must be pinned — this is where vacuity could hide, so treat it as a conjecture until the hypotheses have a witness.

### Lean design (the shared object)

House register (matrices, no group machinery). One new file, `Carrier/EquivariantGradedIndex.lean`:

```
-- shared data: an odd involution and an even commuting element, as Props
def IsOddInvolutionFor  (Γ X : Matrix n n ℂ) : Prop := Γ*Γ = 1 ∧ Γᴴ = Γ ∧ Γ*X = -(X*Γ)
def IsEvenSymmetryFor   (g X Γ : Matrix n n ℂ) : Prop := g*X = X*g ∧ g*Γ = Γ*g
```

Master lemmas, each small:

- **L1** `anticonj_charpoly_eq` — **M, landed**.
- **L2** `balanced_inertia_of_anticonj` — **M-target**; the Aristotle route is already fully specced (call-02 Part A: `charpoly_neg` + `roots_comp_neg_X` + `IsHermitian` eigenvalue multiset + the landed `card_pos_eq_card_neg_of_multiset_map_neg_eq`). Closes clause (i).
- **L3** `graded_supertrace_localizes_to_kernel` — **M-target**, new, the finite McKean–Singer–Lefschetz engine: for Hermitian (or semisimple) `X`, `ΓXΓ = −X`, `[g,X] = [g,Γ] = 0`: `tr(Γg) = tr((Γg)|_{ker X})`. Proof: eigenspace decomposition; `Γ` maps `E_λ → E_{−λ}`, `g` preserves each; off-kernel contributions cancel in pairs. This single lemma *is* clause (ii); `g = 1` re-derives the index protection family.
- **L4** `sector_involution_pinning` — **M-target**, exactly the C4-doc spec (`Submodule.finrank_sup_add_finrank_inf_eq`; no spectral theorem). Clause (iii).
- **L5** the Schur bridge — **C** until hypotheses are witnessed.

Capstone file re-derives every landed corollary as a one-liner and carries a guard block. Grade of the framework claim itself: **MEMO** until L2–L4 land; the claim "RG-Schur is not an instance" is **MEMO** (structural argument, above).

## Part B — the S6 color-singlet mass-budget witness (concrete design)

**First, a no-go that must shape the design (this is the key finding of this part).** The ask's "single-fiber singlet" (`Λ⁰ ⊕ Λ³` of one `Λ(ℂ³)`) provably gives `b_C = 0` for *any* transports acting through the color representation: the singlet line has **multiplicity one**, so (Schur) every invariant operator acts there as a scalar; and for one-leg background transports the singlet expectation functional is the normalized trace, which kills the commutator `K`. Same for a maximally-entangled meson with covariant pair-transport (`v ⊗ v̄`): conjugation-invariance of trace kills it. I verified both vanishings by exact arithmetic this call. **Corollary worth landing as its own mini-theorem** (`singlet_one_leg_closure_zero`, M-target): `b_C ≠ 0` requires the singlet to *stretch over a non-flat holonomy loop*. This makes the witness principled, not tuned — the closure share of a singlet is literally a **difference of Wilson loops**, zero on flat backgrounds.

### The witness (all entries rational; verified by exact-fraction oracle this call)

**Space** `V = ℂ² ⊗ (ℂ³ ⊗ ℂ̄³)`, dim 18, indexed `Fin 2 × (Fin 3 × Fin 3)` (spinor ⊗ quark ⊗ antiquark).

**Operators** (Kronecker products; `1ₖ` = identity):

- Clifford null pair: `γ₁ = E₀₁ ⊗ 1₉`, `γ₂ = E₁₀ ⊗ 1₉`; metric `g = !![0,1;1,0]` (checks `hcl`: `γᵢ² = 0`, `{γ₁,γ₂} = 1`).
- Grading `Γ = σ_z ⊗ 1₉`; turn `φ = 1₁₈` (so `hGammaSq`, `hGammaAnti`, `hPhiGamma`, `hPhiComm`, `hCov` are immediate).
- Transports (3-4-5 rational rotations `u = R_z`, `v = R_x`, `w = R_y`, e.g. `u = (1/5)!![3,−4,0; 4,3,0; 0,0,5]`):
  `∇₁ = 1₂ ⊗ (u ⊗ 1₃)` (edge 1 moves the quark), `∇₂ = 1₂ ⊗ (v ⊗ w̄)` (edge 2 moves both ends along *different* links — `w̄ = w` since real). Non-commuting: `K = [∇₁,∇₂] = 1₂ ⊗ ([u,v] ⊗ w) ≠ 0`, so `Q_C = 2 σ_z ⊗ ([u,v] ⊗ w) ≠ 0`.

**State** `ψ = χ ⊗ s`, `χ = (1,0)` (spin-up), `s = Σₐ eₐ ⊗ fₐ` (unnormalized ε-singlet; normalization cancels in shares). `ev = ⟨ψ, ·ψ⟩` (Hilbert functional — see trap 3).

**Predicted budget** (exact; key identity `⟨s|A⊗B|s⟩ = Tr(A Bᵀ)`):

```text
ev(Q_C) = 2·Tr([u,v]wᵀ) = 2·(71/125 − 199/125) = −256/125   (a Wilson-loop difference)
ev(Q_A) = 2·Tr((uv+vu)wᵀ) = 540/125
4·ev(Q_T) = 4·‖ψ‖² = 12
M² = 1784/125,   (b_A, b_C, b_T) = (135/446, −32/223, 375/446),  sum = 1 ✓
```

**b_C ≠ 0 and negative** — the balanced-closure sign structure, exhibited. Bonus prediction: the spin-down state `χ = (0,1)` flips the closure sign: `(b_A, b_C, b_T) = (135/574, +32/287, 375/574)` with `M²(↓) − M²(↑) = 512/125 > 0` — an honest finite **hyperfine (π/ρ-analog) splitting**, the spin-aligned/anti-aligned pair split by the chromomagnetic channel alone. Land both states plus the flat negative control (`∇₂ = 1₂ ⊗ (v ⊗ v̄)` ⇒ `ev(Q_C) = 0` exactly).

**Convention traps (flag all five in the module docstring):**

1. **`Λ(ℂ³)` vs `ℂ³⊗ℂ̄³`**: the antiquark leg is `Λ²(ℂ³)` via the ε/Hodge map — sign trap `⋆e₂ = e₃∧e₁` (not `e₁∧e₃`), and `Λ²(g) ≅ ḡ` only for `det g = 1`. The two-fiber realization above is the safe concrete model.
2. **XOR-Fano**: do *not* build the singlet from octonion basis products; Fano orientation signs differ from wedge signs. Strand-count only (`{e001,e010,e100} = Λ¹` etc.); anything octonionic goes through `ConventionBridge`.
3. **Krein `#` vs Hilbert `†`**: `ev` here is Hilbert. The Krein functional `⟨ψ, J·ψ⟩` with `J = σ_z⊗1` (which *is* the closure bivector — Part A again) coincides for spin-up but not in general; the docstring must name the functional, and prose must not call `ev(D²)` "the mass" (see Part C).
4. **Real transports don't probe `3` vs `3̄`**: with complex `SU(3)` entries the antiquark factor is `w̄` and the color trace is `Tr([u,v]w†)` — keep the conjugate explicit.
5. **Proof route**: discharge `carrier_square_assembly` hypotheses structurally via Kronecker lemmas (`Matrix.kroneckerMap` API), evaluating only 3×3 traces by `norm_num` — do not `fin_cases` an 18×18 matrix.

Grades: hypothesis discharge + budget values = **M-target** (rational `decide`/`norm_num`; numbers oracle-verified this call, so currently **C** with the kill condition "exact Lean arithmetic disagrees with `(135/446, −32/223, 375/446)`"); the one-leg/multiplicity-one no-go = **M-target**; the hyperfine reading = **MEMO**.

## Part C — the single weakest load-bearing link

**It is the mass functional.** Not `Q_A` vs `det P` (that weld is further along than the prompt suggests: `Q_A_eq_totalSq` already pins `Q_A = Q(Σα)` at unit transports, with a specced lane to `NBodyAperture`); not channel canonicity (the split is symmetry-typed — symmetric vs antisymmetric under `e↔f`, plus the scalar turn — a referee can be answered); not finite-to-continuum (explicitly disclaimed). The vulnerability is that **"mass" appears in the manuscript with two non-identified meanings**: in §3 mass is `det P` of a state's momentum (spectral/invariant, trusted); from §4 on, "mass" is `M² := 4·ev(D²)` — the expectation of an operator square in a *chosen* state against a *chosen* linear functional. Every headline downstream (budget shares, the S6 witness above, the hyperfine splitting, the Banks–Casher reading) uses the expectation meaning while borrowing prestige from the determinant meaning. A sharp referee will say: *an expectation of `D²` is a mass only at an eigenstate; on a Krein space it is not even variationally meaningful without positivity — so the four-channel budget decomposes a quadratic functional, not a mass.* That is the deepest conceptual hole, and both §4 rails leave it open.

**The one shoring-up theorem** — and the just-landed `aperture_dominance_pos` makes it ripe:

> **`sector_ground_mass` (finite Rayleigh–Ritz keystone, M-target).** Hypotheses: a sector `P` that is `J`-positive-definite and on which the dominance inequality of `aperture_dominance_pos` holds for `J·(Q_A+Q_C)` (trap 3(a) above), with `D` Krein-self-adjoint. Conclusions: (a) `D^#D|_P` is diagonalizable with real spectrum bounded below by `c − κ > 0` in the `J`-inner product; (b) `M²_spec := min spec(D^#D|_P) = min_{ψ∈P∖0} 4·ev_ψ(D²)/‖ψ‖²_J`; (c) at a minimizer `ψ₀`, the signed budget shares decompose `M²_spec`.

One theorem, three payoffs: it converts `ev(D²)` from a chosen functional into the spectral mass of the sector ground state (the referee's objection dies exactly there); it gives the budget its referee-proof sentence ("the *ground-state* mass decomposes into signed channel shares"); and it is precisely the precondition S5 (first-meson) needs, connecting to the OS/transfer-gap pillars so the same number has an operational strong-coupling meaning. It is finite linear algebra — Mathlib's `IsHermitian.spectral_theorem` plus a Courant–Fischer-style min characterization — squarely in Aristotle's range, with rung 3 of the dominance ladder (the rational 6×6 `c, κ` computation) supplying the first non-vacuous witness. This is the arch keystone: §3 and §4 stop being two solid pillars joined by prose and become one arch.
The background search only confirmed the two context-core files I had already located and read in full (`C4_SECTORED_INDEX_AND_STRATEGY.md` and `COLLABORATOR_BRIEF_2026-07-08.md` under `AgentTasks/overnight-allmass-run-2026-07-08/`). No new material — the review above stands as delivered, grounded in those docs plus the verbatim Lean sources.

- **Part A**: master finite equivariant-index theorem (three clauses: odd-element balance, kernel-localized Lefschetz supertrace, sector pinning) unifying McKean–Singer, C4, and S1-CC as literal corollaries; RG-Schur excluded with the structural reason, attached via the `schur_complement_preserves_sector_index` bridge (C); Lean design L1–L5 with L1 already landed.
- **Part B**: 18-dim quark–antiquark ε-singlet witness with rational 3-4-5 rotation transports; exact budget `(135/446, −32/223, 375/446)` verified by fraction oracle this call; the multiplicity-one/one-leg Schur no-go (`b_C = 0` forced) as a mandatory companion mini-theorem; five convention traps flagged; hyperfine spin-flip control `M²(↓) − M²(↑) = 512/125`.
- **Part C**: weakest link = the two non-identified meanings of "mass" (`det P` vs `4·ev(D²)`); shoring theorem = `sector_ground_mass`, the finite Rayleigh–Ritz keystone on the aperture-dominance sector.

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01TQxmhDKopeh8Joain7qoz1","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# The null-edge mass program: status and hardest challenges\n2\t\n3\tA standalone brief for external collaborators, 2026-07-08. No access to\n4\tour repository is assumed; everything needed is defined here. We are\n5\tasking for guidance on the seven challenges in section 5. Please read\n6\tsection 6 (pitfalls and closed routes) before proposing approaches -\n7\tseveral natural ideas are already dead by proof or measurement.\n8\t\n9\t## 1. What this project is\n10\t\n11\tA machine-verified physics program: we formalize, in Lean 4 (Mathlib,\n12\tpinned toolchain), the thesis that **mass is the obstruction to coherent\n13\tnull transport** - matter is trapped, mutually disagreeing light. The\n14\tLean kernel is our source of truth. Every claim carries a grade:\n15\t**T** (source-verified theorem), **M** (machine/kernel-verified),\n16\t**MEMO** (expert-verified prose, kernel transcription pending),\n17\t**C** (pre-registered conjecture with explicit kill conditions),\n18\t**[import]** (external input). We pre-register tests before running\n19\tthem, and we report kills as loudly as wins - two long-standing\n20\tconjectures died this week by their own pre-registered criteria, and we\n21\tcount that as the system working.\n22\t\n23\t## 2. The trusted core (kernel-checked, no caveats)\n24\t\n25\tFor 2-component Weyl spinors `psi_1..psi_n` (null momenta\n26\t`psi_i psi_i^dag`), with `P = sum_i psi_i psi_i^dag`:\n27\t\n28\t```text\n29\tdet P = sum_{i<j} |psi_i wedge psi_j|^2\n30\t```\n31\t\n32\tInvariant mass squared IS total pairwise null-direction disagreement;\n33\tzero iff all directions collinear. Machine-checked, no axioms beyond\n34\tthe standard three. Everything else orbits this.\n35\t\n36\t## 3. The formal architecture (one paragraph each)\n37\t\n38\t**Carrier.** A finite 2-complex (vertices, edges, faces) carrying a\n39\tDirac-type operator `D = sum_e c(alpha_e) nabla_e + Gamma phi`: null\n40\tcovector solderings `alpha_e` (Clifford coefficients `c(alpha)^2 = 0`),\n41\tcovariant edge transports `nabla_e`, and a vertex \"turn\" term (the\n42\tHiggs-shaped channel). A kernel-checked Weitzenboeck identity splits\n43\t`4 D^#D` into four channels: aperture `Q_A` (kinetic, ties to det P),\n44\tclosure `Q_C` (gauge/QCD-shaped: commutators of transports), turn `Q_T`\n45\t(Yukawa-shaped), soldering-gradient `E` (gravity-shaped). Slogan:\n46\tunification is decomposition. `#` is a Krein-space adjoint - the\n47\tgeometry is indefinite by construction, and positivity holds (at best)\n48\tonly on a constrained physical sector, Gupta-Bleuler style.\n49\t\n50\t**Protection.** Finite McKean-Singer index theorems (kernel-checked):\n51\tan unbalanced chirality count forces exact massless modes immune to\n52\tevery potential and transport.\n53\t\n54\t**Dynamics.** Kernel-checked this week: one decimation (Schur\n55\tcomplement) step converts per-edge null structure into non-null\n56\teffective terms with coefficient exactly the pairwise Clifford pairing\n57\t(`(ab)^2 = k(ab)` for square-zero a, b with anticommutator k); the\n58\teffective coupling vanishes identically for collinear pairs. So the\n59\tSAME disagreement invariant that is mass kinematically becomes mass\n60\tdynamically under coarse-graining.\n61\t\n62\t**QCD lane.** Kernel-checked at strong coupling on concrete finite\n63\tlattices: Wilson-loop area law, slab reflection positivity, a\n64\ttransfer/OS spectral gap, exponential clustering, character-expansion\n65\tbounds. Also: the Wilson plaquette action is EXACTLY the squared\n66\tHilbert-Schmidt norm of the closure defect `1 - holonomy` (one-line\n67\tidentity, now pinned). This week's sharpest structural result (verified\n68\tanalytically + by two independent numerical oracles, Lean transcription\n69\tin progress): the nonabelian closure channel is an EXACT Krein square,\n70\t`Q_C = L^# L`, with an explicit gauge-covariant current\n71\t`L = c(alpha_1) (x) 1 + c(alpha_2) (x) (-K/2)`, `K = [nabla_1,nabla_2]`,\n72\tfor ANY compact group - but Krein squares carry no positivity by\n73\tthemselves, which relocates the whole positivity question (challenge\n74\tC2).\n75\t\n76\t## 4. Status snapshot (end of 2026-07-07)\n77\t\n78\t- Kernel-checked: the section-2 theorem; the 4-channel Weitzenboeck\n79\t  split; index-protection family; the decimation/mass-generation\n80\t  witness with its collinear control; the strong-coupling QCD stack\n81\t  above; exact finite Ginsparg-Wilson chirality structure whose grading\n82\t  is edge-orientation reversal; a nonvacuous positive Gupta-Bleuler\n83\t  quotient witness on a small model; color-fiber structure (particle\n84\t  states as exterior-algebra \"strand\" monomials; hypercharge derived;\n85\t  lepton/baryon number = strand-count grading).\n86\t- MEMO grade: Standard-Model representation selection (five-strand\n87\t  principle); three generations via triality-as-monodromy (multiplicity\n88\t  menu {1,3} forced); equipartition trace identity behind the Koide\n89\t  combination.\n90\t- KILLED this week (pre-registered probes): see section 6.\n91\t- DISCOVERED this week: (i) every cyclically symmetric celestial\n92\t  decoration of a transport cycle pins an EXACT zero quasi-energy mode\n93\t  (generic decorations sit near zero); geometric decorations self-lock\n94\t  onto the abstract \"massless locus\"; (ii) the closure channel is\n95\t  chromomagnetic (linear in field strength), NOT energy-shaped - see\n96\t  pitfall P2.\n97\t\n98\t## 5. The hardest challenges (the ask)\n99\t\n100\t**C1 - a finite forest-counting injection (pure combinatorics; our\n101\tsingle most-resisted target: five failed expert-prover attempts).**\n102\tSetting: cluster/polymer expansion (Penrose-style tree partition\n103\tscheme). We need: for a fixed rooted-forest target with given block\n104\tsizes `m_j` summing appropriately, an explicit injection from\n105\t{fiber element} x Perm(k) x prod_j Perm(m_j) into orderings of n\n106\tlabeled objects (root-first, blocks in sigma-order, internally\n107\treordered), with fiber constancy - the missing bound is\n108\t`|fiber| * k! * prod m_j! <= n!`. The intended proof is a left-inverse\n109\t(parse the ordering back), with canonical-least-root and\n110\tincreasing-children constraints as tie-breaks. GUIDANCE SOUGHT: a\n111\tclean certificate/encoding argument, or a reference where exactly this\n112\tfiber bound is proved (Penrose 1967 descendants; Fernandez-Procacci),\n113\tor a reformulation that avoids the injection entirely.\n114\t\n115\t**C2 - constraint-compatible representatives in a Krein space (the\n116\tpositivity crux).** The closure channel is an exact Krein square\n117\t`Q_C = L^# L` whose representation family is a GL-torsor\n118\t{`L_A = c(alpha_1)(x)A + c(alpha_2)(x)(A^dag)^{-1}(-K/2)`}. On the FULL\n119\tspace the form `[psi, Q_C psi]` is maximally indefinite (necessarily -\n120\tnull Clifford coefficients are #-isotropic). Positivity can only hold\n121\ton a physical sector `V'/N` (Gauss-law kernel modulo null vectors), and\n122\tdescends IF some torsor member preserves `V'` and its constraint\n123\tsubspace. QUESTION: for lattice gauge Gauss constraints, is there a\n124\tprincipled construction of a constraint-compatible representative\n125\t(e.g. via gauge averaging, BRST-style doublets, or a positive-definite\n126\tdeformation)? Any literature on \"operator squares in Krein spaces\n127\trestricted to Pontryagin-positive sectors\"? A no-go would also be\n128\tvaluable: if the restricted form must stay indefinite, chiral symmetry\n129\tbreaking wants it that way (the curvature term must pull eigenvalues\n130\tdown), and we need the sharp statement of that trade-off.\n131\t\n132\t**C3 - multi-direction closure representation.** The exact square above\n133\tis proved for TWO transport directions. With d >= 3 directions,\n134\t`Q_C = -sum_{mu<nu} b_{mu nu} (x) [nabla_mu, nabla_nu]` and per-pair\n135\tcurrents generate cross-pair symmetric contaminations. CONJECTURE\n136\t(pre-registered): a compensated form `Q_C + (aperture-shaped\n137\tcorrection) = L^# L`. No access to\nour repository is assumed; everything needed is defined here. We are\nasking for guidance on the seven challenges in section 5. Please read\nsection 6 (pitfalls and closed routes) before proposing approaches -\nseveral natural ideas are already dead by proof or measurement.\n\n## 1. What this project is\n\nA machine-verified physics program: we formalize, in Lean 4 (Mathlib,\npinned toolchain), the thesis that **mass is the obstruction to coherent\nnull transport** - matter is trapped, mutually disagreeing light. The\nLean kernel is our source of truth. Every claim carries a grade:\n**T** (source-verified theorem), **M** (machine/kernel-verified),\n**MEMO** (expert-verified prose, kernel transcription pending),\n**C** (pre-registered conjecture with explicit kill conditions),\n**[import]** (external input). We pre-register tests before running\nthem, and we report kills as loudly as wins - two long-standing\nconjectures died this week by their own pre-registered criteria, and we\ncount that as the system working.\n\n## 2. The trusted core (kernel-checked, no caveats)\n\nFor 2-component Weyl spinors `psi_1..psi_n` (null momenta\n`psi_i psi_i^dag`), with `P = sum_i psi_i psi_i^dag`:\n\n```text\ndet P = sum_{i<j} |psi_i wedge psi_j|^2\n```\n\nInvariant mass squared IS total pairwise null-direction disagreement;\nzero iff all directions collinear. Machine-checked, no axioms beyond\nthe standard three. Everything else orbits this.\n\n## 3. The formal architecture (one paragraph each)\n\n**Carrier.** A finite 2-complex (vertices, edges, faces) carrying a\nDirac-type operator `D = sum_e c(alpha_e) nabla_e + Gamma phi`: null\ncovector solderings `alpha_e` (Clifford coefficients `c(alpha)^2 = 0`),\ncovariant edge transports `nabla_e`, and a vertex \"turn\" term (the\nHiggs-shaped channel). A kernel-checked Weitzenboeck identity splits\n`4 D^#D` into four channels: aperture `Q_A` (kinetic, ties to det P),\nclosure `Q_C` (gauge/QCD-shaped: commutators of transports), turn `Q_T`\n(Yukawa-shaped), soldering-gradient `E` (gravity-shaped). Slogan:\nunification is decomposition. `#` is a Krein-space adjoint - the\ngeometry is indefinite by construction, and positivity holds (at best)\nonly on a constrained physical sector, Gupta-Bleuler style.\n\n**Protection.** Finite McKean-Singer index theorems (kernel-checked):\nan unbalanced chirality count forces exact massless modes immune to\nevery potential and transport.\n\n**Dynamics.** Kernel-checked this week: one decimation (Schur\ncomplement) step converts per-edge null structure into non-null\neffective terms with coefficient exactly the pairwise Clifford pairing\n(`(ab)^2 = k(ab)` for square-zero a, b with anticommutator k); the\neffective coupling vanishes identically for collinear pairs. So the\nSAME disagreement invariant that is mass kinematically becomes mass\ndynamically under coarse-graining.\n\n**QCD lane.** Kernel-checked at strong coupling on concrete finite\nlattices: Wilson-loop area law, slab reflection positivity, a\ntransfer/OS spectral gap, exponential clustering, character-expansion\nbounds. Also: the Wilson plaquette action is EXACTLY the squared\nHilbert-Schmidt norm of the closure defect `1 - holonomy` (one-line\nidentity, now pinned). This week's sharpest structural result (verified\nanalytically + by two independent numerical oracles, Lean transcription\nin progress): the nonabelian closure channel is an EXACT Krein square,\n`Q_C = L^# L`, with an explicit gauge-covariant current\n`L = c(alpha_1) (x) 1 + c(alpha_2) (x) (-K/2)`, `K = [nabla_1,nabla_2]`,\nfor ANY compact group - but Krein squares carry no positivity by\nthemselves, which relocates the whole positivity question (challenge\nC2).\n\n## 4. Status snapshot (end of 2026-07-07)\n\n- Kernel-checked: the section-2 theorem; the 4-channel Weitzenboeck\n  split; index-protection family; the decimation/mass-generation\n  witness with its collinear control; the strong-coupling QCD stack\n  above; exact finite Ginsparg-Wilson chirality structure whose grading\n  is edge-orientation reversal; a nonvacuous positive Gupta-Bleuler\n  quotient witness on a small model; color-fiber structure (particle\n  states as exterior-algebra \"strand\" monomials; hypercharge derived;\n  lepton/baryon number = strand-count grading).\n- MEMO grade: Standard-Model representation selection (five-strand\n  principle); three generations via triality-as-monodromy (multiplicity\n  menu {1,3} forced); equipartition trace identity behind the Koide\n  combination.\n- KILLED this week (pre-registered probes): see section 6.\n- DISCOVERED this week: (i) every cyclically symmetric celestial\n  decoration of a transport cycle pins an EXACT zero quasi-energy mode\n  (generic decorations sit near zero); geometric decorations self-lock\n  onto the abstract \"massless locus\"; (ii) the closure channel is\n  chromomagnetic (linear in field strength), NOT energy-shaped - see\n  pitfall P2.\n\n## 5. The hardest challenges (the ask)\n\n**C1 - a finite forest-counting injection (pure combinatorics; our\nsingle most-resisted target: five failed expert-prover attempts).**\nSetting: cluster/polymer expansion (Penrose-style tree partition\nscheme). We need: for a fixed rooted-forest target with given block\nsizes `m_j` summing appropriately, an explicit injection from\n{fiber element} x Perm(k) x prod_j Perm(m_j) into orderings of n\nlabeled objects (root-first, blocks in sigma-order, internally\nreordered), with fiber constancy - the missing bound is\n`|fiber| * k! * prod m_j! <= n!`. The intended proof is a left-inverse\n(parse the ordering back), with canonical-least-root and\nincreasing-children constraints as tie-breaks. GUIDANCE SOUGHT: a\nclean certificate/encoding argument, or a reference where exactly this\nfiber bound is proved (Penrose 1967 descendants; Fernandez-Procacci),\nor a reformulation that avoids the injection entirely.\n\n**C2 - constraint-compatible representatives in a Krein space (the\npositivity crux).** The closure channel is an exact Krein square\n`Q_C = L^# L` whose representation family is a GL-torsor\n{`L_A = c(alpha_1)(x)A + c(alpha_2)(x)(A^dag)^{-1}(-K/2)`}. On the FULL\nspace the form `[psi, Q_C psi]` is maximally indefinite (necessarily -\nnull Clifford coefficients are #-isotropic). Positivity can only hold\non a physical sector `V'/N` (Gauss-law kernel modulo null vectors), and\ndescends IF some torsor member preserves `V'` and its constraint\nsubspace. QUESTION: for lattice gauge Gauss constraints, is there a\nprincipled construction of a constraint-compatible representative\n(e.g. via gauge averaging, BRST-style doublets, or a positive-definite\ndeformation)? Any literature on \"operator squares in Krein spaces\nrestricted to Pontryagin-positive sectors\"? A no-go would also be\nvaluable: if the restricted form must stay indefinite, chiral symmetry\nbreaking wants it that way (the curvature term must pull eigenvalues\ndown), and we need the sharp statement of that trade-off.\n\n**C3 - multi-direction closure representation.** The exact square above\nis proved for TWO transport directions. With d >= 3 directions,\n`Q_C = -sum_{mu<nu} b_{mu nu} (x) [nabla_mu, nabla_nu]` and per-pair\ncurrents generate cross-pair symmetric contaminations. CONJECTURE\n(pre-registered): a compensated form `Q_C + (aperture-shaped\ncorrection) = L^# L`. GUIDANCE: is there a known algebraic identity\n(Fierz-like, or from Clifford bimodule theory) organizing\n`sum_pairs (bivector) (x) (curvature)` as a single square plus a\ncanonical symmetric remainder?\n\n**C4 - the right invariant for symmetry-forced zero modes.** Finding:\na length-V cycle of SU(2) frame transports built from V celestial\ndirections arranged symmetrically on ANY cone pins an exact unit\neigenvalue of the (unitary) transfer; random decorations give only\nnear-zero modes; in abstract (non-geometric) data the zero mode needs a\ncodim-1 locus relating amplitude and Berry-phase holonomy - which\ngeometric decorations satisfy AUTOMATICALLY. Discrete-time quantum-walk\ntopology (Kitagawa et al.; Asboth et al.: 0- and pi-quasienergy\ninvariants) is clearly adjacent but is about boundary states of walks,\nnot decoration-forced spectral pinning on closed loops. QUESTION: what\nis the correct invariant/theorem class here - equivariant index on the\ncircle? spectral flow with a symmetry constraint? Kramers-like pinning\nfrom an antiunitary in the cyclic group's presence?\n\n**C5 - is any honest mass-VALUE route still alive?** Our Koide-relation\nmechanism died by its own pre-registered probe: the dynamically\nmeasured soldering coefficient is kappa = 3/2 (predicting Q = 5/9),\nnot the kappa = 1 (Q = 2/3) the relation needs, and the reduction does\nnot even produce the uniform-diagonal ansatz. Any surviving route must\nALSO clear Sumino's bar (family symmetry protecting the relation\nagainst QED running). QUESTION: given a finite mechanism that produces\nmass RATIOS from cycle geometry (spectra like pi/6 {0,1,2} arise\nnaturally), what observable targets are honest at finite level -\nneutrino mass-squared-difference ratios? Koide-like relations for other\nsectors? Or should finite programs stop at structural statements?\n\n**C6 - what continuum statements are realistically provable?** Our\nrule: quotient-then-limit (algebraic quotients at finite level, limits\nonly through a benchmark ladder of refinement maps; the exact GW\nstructure and a Wilson-term identity are the anchors). QUESTION: for a\nprogram that will NOT attempt constructive Yang-Mills, what is the\nstrongest continuum-facing theorem class worth targeting - graph/\noperator convergence of Dirac spectra under refinement (Laplacian\nrenormalization-group style), Gromov-Hausdorff-type spectral\nconvergence, or categorical colimits of transfer data?\n\n**C7 - discrete torsion split of the gravity channel.** The\nsoldering-gradient channel E is NOT pure discrete torsion (our earlier\nconjecture died by probe). Pre-registered target: an exact split\n`2E = Contract(T) + Contract(S)` with T the antisymmetrized soldering\ndifference (torsion-shaped) and S a symmetric remainder\n(non-metricity-shaped) - a finite \"geometric trinity\" statement.\nGUIDANCE: pointers to teleparallel/symmetric-teleparallel\ndecompositions done at simplicial/discrete level (Regge-adjacent),\nand the right discrete definition of contorsion.\n\n## 6. Closed routes and pitfalls (do not spend time here)\n\n- P1. \"Q_C = sum over faces of (1 - holonomy)^dag (1 - holonomy)\"\n  (site-diagonal defect Gram) is DEAD structurally: the closure channel\n  is purely off-diagonal in the site grading; any site-local Gram is\n  exactly Frobenius-orthogonal to it. (The defect Gram is a fine object\n  - it is the Wilson ACTION - it is just not this operator.)\n- P2. Do not conflate the two closure objects: the defect GRAM is\n  |F|^2-shaped (energy); the Weitzenboeck closure CHANNEL is linear in\n  F (chromomagnetic, sigma.F). In mass-budget language the channel's\n  share is hyperfine-shaped (Delta-N, rho-pi splittings), not \"99\n  percent of the proton\".\n- P3. Koide via tetrahedral corner geometry: killed by measurement\n  (kappa = 3/2). The equipartition trace identity survives as algebra.\n- P4. \"Trace of E = discrete torsion\": killed by probe.\n- P5. Retardedness alone cannot delete fermion doublers (proved:\n  determinant-level obstruction); one-sided Ginsparg-Wilson inversion\n  is false in nonabelian settings (explicit 2x2 counterexample) -\n  palindromic/midpoint transfer ordering is the correct convention.\n- P6. Spectral-measure language is embargoed program-wide until the C2\n  positivity question resolves; finite eigenvalue-COUNT identities are\n  the sanctioned form (e.g. our finite Banks-Casher target\n  `m V Sigma_m = N_m`).\n- P7. We never claim continuum existence, physical scale (dimensional\n  transmutation), or anything Clay-adjacent; ratios and finite\n  identities only.\n\n## 7. How to respond\n\nMemo format preferred: numbered findings; explicit claim labels\n(theorem / heuristic / reference / conjecture); kill conditions for any\nmechanism you propose; references with enough precision to locate the\nexact lemma (we verify against full text, not abstracts). Partial\nanswers to single challenges are very welcome. If you believe a\nchallenge is malposed, saying WHY is as valuable as solving it - our\nbest week yet came from two well-aimed kills.\n","numLines":224,"startLine":1,"totalLines":224}}}
