{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01KH8bjZ9Y4vNZB3FJjBnAYg","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t/-\n2\t# S1-CC: closure is balanced (not positive) on the physical sector\n3\t\n4\tDRAFT (kernel-clean; no `s o r r y`). The kernel core of the Fable call-01\n5\tresolution of the central positivity crux (S1-CC), overnight all-mass run\n6\t2026-07-08. The full analysis is in the call log\n7\t(`AgentTasks/model-calls/claude/2026-07-07-231939-fable-call-01.md`,\n8\tPart B) and summarized in the QCD roadmap.\n9\t\n10\t## The finding (grade MEMO for the physics, M for this file)\n11\t\n12\tThe nonabelian closure channel is an exact Krein square `Q_C = L^# L`\n13\t(kernel-checked elsewhere), but Krein squares carry no positivity. The\n14\tcrux \"is closure positive on the physical sector `V'/N`?\" is resolved:\n15\t**no - it is exactly BALANCED (Krein signature zero), structurally.** The\n16\tmechanism is a grading anticonjugation: the closure bivector\n17\t`b = sigma_z (x) 1` satisfies `b^{-1} (J Q_C) b = -(J Q_C)` and preserves\n18\tevery gauge-defined constraint sector (gauge acts on the color/site factor\n19\talone, commuting with `b`). A matrix congruent to its own negative has\n20\tbalanced inertia (`n_+ = n_-`). So closure positivity holds only vacuously\n21\t(flat Gauss sector); otherwise the channel is honestly signed - which is\n22\texactly what chiral symmetry breaking needs (§8 of the manuscript).\n23\t\n24\t## What this file proves (the house-style rung)\n25\t\n26\t`anticonj_odd_pow_trace_zero`: if an invertible `S` anticonjugates `B`\n27\t(`S^{-1} B S = -B`), then every ODD power of `B` is traceless. This is the\n28\tspectral-symmetry engine behind the balanced inertia: all odd moments of\n29\tthe Hermitian form vanish, so its eigenvalues are symmetric about zero. A\n30\tpure finite trace identity in the same register as `banks_casher_count` -\n31\tno inertia API, no spectral theorem. The inertia reading itself (via\n32\t`charpoly` congruence and Sylvester) and the concrete `V'` construction\n33\tare the next rungs (see the roadmap); this is the load-bearing algebra.\n34\t\n35\t## Claim boundary\n36\t\n37\t`anticonj_odd_pow_trace_zero` is kernel-checked (M). The identification of\n38\t`B = J Q_C` and `S = b` on the concrete carrier physical sector, and the\n39\tstep from odd-moment-vanishing to balanced inertia, are MEMO (Fable Part\n40\tB), pending their own Lean rungs. No positivity is claimed; the point is\n41\tprecisely that closure is NOT positive on the sector.\n42\t\n43\t## Provenance\n44\t\n45\tFable-5 call-01 (2026-07-08), Part B - the crux resolution and the\n46\trecommended house-style rung - [orig]/[interp]; the trace-conjugation and\n47\todd-power algebra is standard - [import].\n48\t-/\n49\t\n50\timport Mathlib\n51\t\n52\tnamespace PhysicsSM.Draft.NullEdge.GateYM.S1CCBalancedInertia\n53\t\n54\topen Matrix\n55\t\n56\tvariable {n : Type*} [Fintype n] [DecidableEq n]\n57\t\n58\t/-- Trace is invariant under conjugation by an invertible matrix:\n59\t`Tr(S⁻¹ X S) = Tr X`. -/\n60\ttheorem trace_invOf_conj (S X : Matrix n n ℂ) [Invertible S] :\n61\t    (⅟S * X * S).trace = X.trace := by\n62\t  rw [Matrix.trace_mul_comm, ← mul_assoc, mul_invOf_self, one_mul]\n63\t\n64\t/-- Conjugation distributes over powers: `(S⁻¹ B S)^m = S⁻¹ B^m S`. -/\n65\ttheorem conj_pow (S B : Matrix n n ℂ) [Invertible S] (m : ℕ) :\n66\t    (⅟S * B * S) ^ m = ⅟S * B ^ m * S := by\n67\t  induction m with\n68\t  | zero => simp\n69\t  | succ m ih =>\n70\t    rw [pow_succ, ih, pow_succ]\n71\t    have hreassoc : (⅟S * B ^ m * S) * (⅟S * B * S)\n72\t        = ⅟S * B ^ m * (S * ⅟S) * B * S := by noncomm_ring\n73\t    rw [hreassoc, mul_invOf_self, mul_one]\n74\t    noncomm_ring\n75\t\n76\t/-- **Anticonjugation kills odd-power traces (S1-CC flagship).** If an\n77\tinvertible `S` anticonjugates `B` (`S⁻¹ * B * S = -B`), then every odd\n78\tpower of `B` is traceless: `Tr(B^(2k+1)) = 0`. The odd moments of the\n79\tassociated Hermitian form all vanish, forcing a spectrum symmetric about\n80\tzero - the engine behind the BALANCED Krein inertia of the closure channel\n81\ton the physical sector (closure is signed, not positive). -/\n82\ttheorem anticonj_odd_pow_trace_zero (B S : Matrix n n ℂ) [Invertible S]\n83\t    (h : ⅟S * B * S = -B) (k : ℕ) :\n84\t    (B ^ (2 * k + 1)).trace = 0 := by\n85\t  have h1 : ((-B) ^ (2 * k + 1)).trace = (B ^ (2 * k + 1)).trace := by\n86\t    rw [← h, conj_pow, trace_invOf_conj]\n87\t  have h2 : ((-B) ^ (2 * k + 1)).trace = -(B ^ (2 * k + 1)).trace := by\n88\t    rw [Odd.neg_pow ⟨k, rfl⟩, Matrix.trace_neg]\n89\t  have key : -(B ^ (2 * k + 1)).trace = (B ^ (2 * k + 1)).trace := h2 ▸ h1\n90\t  linear_combination (-(1 : ℂ) / 2) * key\n91\t\n92\t/-- **Even-power form of the same fact:** for a self-adjoint `B` (`Bᴴ = B`)\n93\tthat is anticonjugate to itself, the anticonjugation gives a spectrum\n94\tsymmetric under negation. Recorded as the companion identity: `Tr B = 0`\n95\t(the `k = 0` case), the vanishing of the first moment. -/\n96\ttheorem anticonj_trace_zero (B S : Matrix n n ℂ) [Invertible S]\n97\t    (h : ⅟S * B * S = -B) : B.trace = 0 := by\n98\t  have := anticonj_odd_pow_trace_zero B S h 0\n99\t  simpa using this\n100\t\n101\t/-- **Spectrum symmetric under negation (the balanced-inertia essence).**\n102\tAnticonjugation `⅟S * B * S = -B` makes `B` similar to `-B`, so their\n103\tcharacteristic polynomials agree: `(-B).charpoly = B.charpoly`. Hence the\n104\teigenvalue multiset of `B` is invariant under `lambda -> -lambda` - the\n105\tpolynomial-level statement that the spectrum is symmetric about zero, from\n106\twhich `n_+ = n_-` (balanced Krein inertia) follows for Hermitian `B` by\n107\tcounting. This is the charpoly rung of the balanced-inertia theorem. -/\n108\ttheorem anticonj_charpoly_eq (B S : Matrix n n ℂ) [Invertible S]\n109\t    (h : ⅟S * B * S = -B) : (-B).charpoly = B.charpoly := by\n110\t  rw [← h]\n111\t  exact Matrix.charpoly_units_conj' (unitOfInvertible S) B\n112\t\n113\t/-! ## Count helper for the balanced-inertia capstone -/\n114\t\n115\t/-- A real multiset invariant under negation has as many positive entries as\n116\tnegative entries, counted with multiplicity.\n117\t\n118\tThis is the finite combinatorial half of the balanced-inertia capstone. The\n119\tremaining spectral bridge is to prove that the Hermitian eigenvalue multiset is\n120\tnegation-invariant from the characteristic-polynomial symmetry. -/\n121\ttheorem countP_pos_eq_countP_neg_of_map_neg_eq (s : Multiset ℝ)\n122\t    (h : s.map Neg.neg = s) :\n123\t    s.countP (fun x => 0 < x) = s.countP (fun x => x < 0) := by\n124\t  calc\n125\t    s.countP (fun x => 0 < x) = (s.map Neg.neg).countP (fun x => 0 < x) := by\n126\t      rw [h]\n127\t    _ = s.countP (fun x => 0 < -x) := by\n128\t      rw [Multiset.countP_map]\n129\t      simp [Multiset.countP_eq_card_filter]\n130\t    _ = s.countP (fun x => x < 0) := by\n131\t      apply Multiset.countP_congr rfl\n132\t      intro x _\n133\t      simp\n134\t\n135\t/-- Index-count form of `countP_pos_eq_countP_neg_of_map_neg_eq`.\n136\t\n137\tIf the multiset of real values indexed by a finite type is invariant under\n138\tnegation, the number of positive indexed values equals the number of negative\n139\tindexed values. This matches the count shape needed for Hermitian eigenvalues. -/\n140\ttheorem card_pos_eq_card_neg_of_multiset_map_neg_eq {m : Type*} [Fintype m]\n141\t    (f : m → ℝ)\n142\t    (h : (Finset.univ.val.map f).map Neg.neg = Finset.univ.val.map f) :\n143\t    (Finset.univ.filter (fun i => 0 < f i)).card =\n144\t      (Finset.univ.filter (fun i => f i < 0)).card := by\n145\t  have hm := countP_pos_eq_countP_neg_of_map_neg_eq (Finset.univ.val.map f) h\n146\t  have hpos : (Finset.univ.filter (fun i => 0 < f i)).card =\n147\t      (Finset.univ.val.map f).countP (fun x => 0 < x) := by\n148\t    rw [Multiset.countP_eq_card_filter, Multiset.filter_map]\n149\t    change (Finset.univ.filter (fun i => 0 < f i)).val.card = _\n150\t    rw [Finset.filter_val]\n151\t    simp\n152\t  have hneg : (Finset.univ.filter (fun i => f i < 0)).card =\n153\t      (Finset.univ.val.map f).countP (fun x => x < 0) := by\n154\t    rw [Multiset.countP_eq_card_filter, Multiset.filter_map]\n155\t    change (Finset.univ.filter (fun i => f i < 0)).val.card = _\n156\t    rw [Finset.filter_val]\n157\t    simp\n158\t  rw [hpos, hneg]\n159\t  exact hm\n160\t\n161\t/-! ## Lemma 1: the half-constraint rigidity (Gupta-Bleuler is forced) -/\n162\t\n163\t/-- **Half-constraint rigidity (Fable call-01, Part B, Lemma 1).** For the\n164\tnull pair `c₁ = E₀₁`, `c₂ = E₁₀` on the Clifford factor, a two-covector\n165\tconstraint charge `Q = c₁ ⊗ G₁ + c₂ ⊗ G₂` (here the `2x2`-over-`B` matrix\n166\t`!![0, G₁; G₂, 0]`) is nilpotent iff `G₁ G₂ = 0` AND `G₂ G₁ = 0`. So a\n167\tNILPOTENT (BRST-type) Gauss charge on the hyperbolic pair cannot use both\n168\tnull covectors nontrivially: the Gupta-Bleuler \"impose only half the\n169\tconstraint\" is not a modeling choice - it is FORCED by nilpotency.\n170\t(`Q² = !![G₁G₂, 0; 0, G₂G₁]`.) -/\n171\ttheorem half_constraint_rigidity {B : Type*} [Ring B] (G₁ G₂ : B) :\n172\t    (!![0, G₁; G₂, 0] : Matrix (Fin 2) (Fin 2) B) ^ 2 = 0\n173\t      ↔ G₁ * G₂ = 0 ∧ G₂ * G₁ = 0 := by\n174\t  have hsq : (!![0, G₁; G₂, 0] : Matrix (Fin 2) (Fin 2) B)\n175\t      * !![0, G₁; G₂, 0] = !![G₁ * G₂, 0; 0, G₂ * G₁] := by\n176\t    ext i j\n177\t    fin_cases i <;> fin_cases j <;>\n178\t      simp [Matrix.mul_apply, Fin.sum_univ_two]\n179\t  rw [sq, hsq]\n180\t  constructor\n181\t  · intro hQ\n182\t    refine ⟨?_, ?_⟩\n183\t    · have := congrFun (congrFun hQ 0) 0; simpa using this\n184\t    · have := congrFun (congrFun hQ 1) 1; simpa using this\n185\t  · rintro ⟨h1, h2⟩\n186\t    ext i j\n187\t    fin_cases i <;> fin_cases j <;> simp [h1, h2]\n188\t\n189\tend PhysicsSM.Draft.NullEdge.GateYM.S1CCBalancedInertia\n190\t"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"e6c6e3b0-0265-4770-b1a6-d84ed8d06188","uuid":"b2127190-05ec-42a0-a32f-332bd6dad3a6","timestamp":"2026-07-08T07:30:50.662Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"C:\\Projects\\StandardModel\\PhysicsSM\\Draft\\NullEdge\\GateYM\\S1CCBalancedInertia.lean","content":"/-\n# S1-CC: closure is balanced (not positive) on the physical sector\n\nDRAFT (kernel-clean; no `s o r r y`). The kernel core of the Fable call-01\nresolution of the central positivity crux (S1-CC), overnight all-mass run\n2026-07-08. The full analysis is in the call log\n(`AgentTasks/model-calls/claude/2026-07-07-231939-fable-call-01.md`,\nPart B) and summarized in the QCD roadmap.\n\n## The finding (grade MEMO for the physics, M for this file)\n\nThe nonabelian closure channel is an exact Krein square `Q_C = L^# L`\n(kernel-checked elsewhere), but Krein squares carry no positivity. The\ncrux \"is closure positive on the physical sector `V'/N`?\" is resolved:\n**no - it is exactly BALANCED (Krein signature zero), structurally.** The\nmechanism is a grading anticonjugation: the closure bivector\n`b = sigma_z (x) 1` satisfies `b^{-1} (J Q_C) b = -(J Q_C)` and preserves\nevery gauge-defined constraint sector (gauge acts on the color/site factor\nalone, commuting with `b`). A matrix congruent to its own negative has\nbalanced inertia (`n_+ = n_-`). So closure positivity holds only vacuously\n(flat Gauss sector); otherwise the channel is honestly signed - which is\nexactly what chiral symmetry breaking needs (§8 of the manuscript).\n\n## What this file proves (the house-style rung)\n\n`anticonj_odd_pow_trace_zero`: if an invertible `S` anticonjugates `B`\n(`S^{-1} B S = -B`), then every ODD power of `B` is traceless. This is the\nspectral-symmetry engine behind the balanced inertia: all odd moments of\nthe Hermitian form vanish, so its eigenvalues are symmetric about zero. A\npure finite trace identity in the same register as `banks_casher_count` -\nno inertia API, no spectral theorem. The inertia reading itself (via\n`charpoly` congruence and Sylvester) and the concrete `V'` construction\nare the next rungs (see the roadmap); this is the load-bearing algebra.\n\n## Claim boundary\n\n`anticonj_odd_pow_trace_zero` is kernel-checked (M). The identification of\n`B = J Q_C` and `S = b` on the concrete carrier physical sector, and the\nstep from odd-moment-vanishing to balanced inertia, are MEMO (Fable Part\nB), pending their own Lean rungs. No positivity is claimed; the point is\nprecisely that closure is NOT positive on the sector.\n\n## Provenance\n\nFable-5 call-01 (2026-07-08), Part B - the crux resolution and the\nrecommended house-style rung - [orig]/[interp]; the trace-conjugation and\nodd-power algebra is standard - [import].\n-/\n\nimport Mathlib\n\nnamespace PhysicsSM.Draft.NullEdge.GateYM.S1CCBalancedInertia\n\nopen Matrix\n\nvariable {n : Type*} [Fintype n] [DecidableEq n]\n\n/-- Trace is invariant under conjugation by an invertible matrix:\n`Tr(S⁻¹ X S) = Tr X`. -/\ntheorem trace_invOf_conj (S X : Matrix n n ℂ) [Invertible S] :\n    (⅟S * X * S).trace = X.trace := by\n  rw [Matrix.trace_mul_comm, ← mul_assoc, mul_invOf_self, one_mul]\n\n/-- Conjugation distributes over powers: `(S⁻¹ B S)^m = S⁻¹ B^m S`. -/\ntheorem conj_pow (S B : Matrix n n ℂ) [Invertible S] (m : ℕ) :\n    (⅟S * B * S) ^ m = ⅟S * B ^ m * S := by\n  induction m with\n  | zero => simp\n  | succ m ih =>\n    rw [pow_succ, ih, pow_succ]\n    have hreassoc : (⅟S * B ^ m * S) * (⅟S * B * S)\n        = ⅟S * B ^ m * (S * ⅟S) * B * S := by noncomm_ring\n    rw [hreassoc, mul_invOf_self, mul_one]\n    noncomm_ring\n\n/-- **Anticonjugation kills odd-power traces (S1-CC flagship).** If an\ninvertible `S` anticonjugates `B` (`S⁻¹ * B * S = -B`), then every odd\npower of `B` is traceless: `Tr(B^(2k+1)) = 0`. The odd moments of the\nassociated Hermitian form all vanish, forcing a spectrum symmetric about\nzero - the engine behind the BALANCED Krein inertia of the closure channel\non the physical sector (closure is signed, not positive). -/\ntheorem anticonj_odd_pow_trace_zero (B S : Matrix n n ℂ) [Invertible S]\n    (h : ⅟S * B * S = -B) (k : ℕ) :\n    (B ^ (2 * k + 1)).trace = 0 := by\n  have h1 : ((-B) ^ (2 * k + 1)).trace = (B ^ (2 * k + 1)).trace := by\n    rw [← h, conj_pow, trace_invOf_conj]\n  have h2 : ((-B) ^ (2 * k + 1)).trace = -(B ^ (2 * k + 1)).trace := by\n    rw [Odd.neg_pow ⟨k, rfl⟩, Matrix.trace_neg]\n  have key : -(B ^ (2 * k + 1)).trace = (B ^ (2 * k + 1)).trace := h2 ▸ h1\n  linear_combination (-(1 : ℂ) / 2) * key\n\n/-- **Even-power form of the same fact:** for a self-adjoint `B` (`Bᴴ = B`)\nthat is anticonjugate to itself, the anticonjugation gives a spectrum\nsymmetric under negation. Recorded as the companion identity: `Tr B = 0`\n(the `k = 0` case), the vanishing of the first moment. -/\ntheorem anticonj_trace_zero (B S : Matrix n n ℂ) [Invertible S]\n    (h : ⅟S * B * S = -B) : B.trace = 0 := by\n  have := anticonj_odd_pow_trace_zero B S h 0\n  simpa using this\n\n/-- **Spectrum symmetric under negation (the balanced-inertia essence).**\nAnticonjugation `⅟S * B * S = -B` makes `B` similar to `-B`, so their\ncharacteristic polynomials agree: `(-B).charpoly = B.charpoly`. Hence the\neigenvalue multiset of `B` is invariant under `lambda -> -lambda` - the\npolynomial-level statement that the spectrum is symmetric about zero, from\nwhich `n_+ = n_-` (balanced Krein inertia) follows for Hermitian `B` by\ncounting. This is the charpoly rung of the balanced-inertia theorem. -/\ntheorem anticonj_charpoly_eq (B S : Matrix n n ℂ) [Invertible S]\n    (h : ⅟S * B * S = -B) : (-B).charpoly = B.charpoly := by\n  rw [← h]\n  exact Matrix.charpoly_units_conj' (unitOfInvertible S) B\n\n/-! ## Count helper for the balanced-inertia capstone -/\n\n/-- A real multiset invariant under negation has as many positive entries as\nnegative entries, counted with multiplicity.\n\nThis is the finite combinatorial half of the balanced-inertia capstone. The\nremaining spectral bridge is to prove that the Hermitian eigenvalue multiset is\nnegation-invariant from the characteristic-polynomial symmetry. -/\ntheorem countP_pos_eq_countP_neg_of_map_neg_eq (s : Multiset ℝ)\n    (h : s.map Neg.neg = s) :\n    s.countP (fun x => 0 < x) = s.countP (fun x => x < 0) := by\n  calc\n    s.countP (fun x => 0 < x) = (s.map Neg.neg).countP (fun x => 0 < x) := by\n      rw [h]\n    _ = s.countP (fun x => 0 < -x) := by\n      rw [Multiset.countP_map]\n      simp [Multiset.countP_eq_card_filter]\n    _ = s.countP (fun x => x < 0) := by\n      apply Multiset.countP_congr rfl\n      intro x _\n      simp\n\n/-- Index-count form of `countP_pos_eq_countP_neg_of_map_neg_eq`.\n\nIf the multiset of real values indexed by a finite type is invariant under\nnegation, the number of positive indexed values equals the number of negative\nindexed values. This matches the count shape needed for Hermitian eigenvalues. -/\ntheorem card_pos_eq_card_neg_of_multiset_map_neg_eq {m : Type*} [Fintype m]\n    (f : m → ℝ)\n    (h : (Finset.univ.val.map f).map Neg.neg = Finset.univ.val.map f) :\n    (Finset.univ.filter (fun i => 0 < f i)).card =\n      (Finset.univ.filter (fun i => f i < 0)).card := by\n  have hm := countP_pos_eq_countP_neg_of_map_neg_eq (Finset.univ.val.map f) h\n  have hpos : (Finset.univ.filter (fun i => 0 < f i)).card =\n      (Finset.univ.val.map f).countP (fun x => 0 < x) := by\n    rw [Multiset.countP_eq_card_filter, Multiset.filter_map]\n    change (Finset.univ.filter (fun i => 0 < f i)).val.card = _\n    rw [Finset.filter_val]\n    simp\n  have hneg : (Finset.univ.filter (fun i => f i < 0)).card =\n      (Finset.univ.val.map f).countP (fun x => x < 0) := by\n    rw [Multiset.countP_eq_card_filter, Multiset.filter_map]\n    change (Finset.univ.filter (fun i => f i < 0)).val.card = _\n    rw [Finset.filter_val]\n    simp\n  rw [hpos, hneg]\n  exact hm\n\n/-! ## Lemma 1: the half-constraint rigidity (Gupta-Bleuler is forced) -/\n\n/-- **Half-constraint rigidity (Fable call-01, Part B, Lemma 1).** For the\nnull pair `c₁ = E₀₁`, `c₂ = E₁₀` on the Clifford factor, a two-covector\nconstraint charge `Q = c₁ ⊗ G₁ + c₂ ⊗ G₂` (here the `2x2`-over-`B` matrix\n`!![0, G₁; G₂, 0]`) is nilpotent iff `G₁ G₂ = 0` AND `G₂ G₁ = 0`. So a\nNILPOTENT (BRST-type) Gauss charge on the hyperbolic pair cannot use both\nnull covectors nontrivially: the Gupta-Bleuler \"impose only half the\nconstraint\" is not a modeling choice - it is FORCED by nilpotency.\n(`Q² = !![G₁G₂, 0; 0, G₂G₁]`.) -/\ntheorem half_constraint_rigidity {B : Type*} [Ring B] (G₁ G₂ : B) :\n    (!![0, G₁; G₂, 0] : Matrix (Fin 2) (Fin 2) B) ^ 2 = 0\n      ↔ G₁ * G₂ = 0 ∧ G₂ * G₁ = 0 := by\n  have hsq : (!![0, G₁; G₂, 0] : Matrix (Fin 2) (Fin 2) B)\n      * !![0, G₁; G₂, 0] = !![G₁ * G₂, 0; 0, G₂ * G₁] := by\n    ext i j\n    fin_cases i <;> fin_cases j <;>\n      simp [Matrix.mul_apply, Fin.sum_univ_two]\n  rw [sq, hsq]\n  constructor\n  · intro hQ\n    refine ⟨?_, ?_⟩\n    · have := congrFun (congrFun hQ 0) 0; simpa using this\n    · have := congrFun (congrFun hQ 1) 1; simpa using this\n  · rintro ⟨h1, h2⟩\n    ext i j\n    fin_cases i <;> fin_cases j <;> simp [h1, h2]\n\nend PhysicsSM.Draft.NullEdge.GateYM.S1CCBalancedInertia\n","numLines":190,"startLine":1,"totalLines":190}}}
{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-fable-5","id":"msg_011CcpCWeZSTskuZEjx42v7j","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01HW57sF1pTBYZPBP59CAQgT","name":"Read","input":{"file_path":"C:\\Projects\\StandardModel\\PhysicsSM\\Draft\\NullEdge\\Carrier\\CarrierIndexProtection.lean"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":599,"cache_read_input_tokens":44908,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":599,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":49,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"global"},"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"e6c6e3b0-0265-4770-b1a6-d84ed8d06188","uuid":"c006bb6a-b8e6-4a02-8f73-2c04c75a02b2"}
{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01HW57sF1pTBYZPBP59CAQgT","type":"tool_result","content":"1\timport Mathlib\n2\t\n3\t/-!\n4\t# Index protection of massless chiral modes: the finite McKean-Singer mechanism\n5\t\n6\tThe mass thesis explains what pushes states OFF the light cone; this file formalizes the\n7\tconverse jewel — what must STAY on it. For a chirality-odd carrier written in Weyl block\n8\tform `D = [[0, D₋], [D₊, 0]]` on `M = M₊ ⊕ M₋` (the `Γ = ±1` sectors), the **chiral\n9\tindex**\n10\t\n11\t>  `ind(D) := dim ker D₊ − dim ker D₋`\n12\t\n13\tcounts massless positive-chirality modes minus massless negative-chirality modes (for a\n14\tcarrier, `ker D₊ = ker D ∩ M₊` is precisely the massless chiral sector). The finite\n15\tMcKean-Singer mechanism is pure rank-nullity: whenever the two blocks have equal rank,\n16\t\n17\t>  `ind(D) = dim M₊ − dim M₋`,\n18\t\n19\ta number fixed by the complex alone. The rank hypothesis is **automatic** for\n20\t* Hilbert-self-adjoint carriers (`D₋ = D₊†`, since `rank f† = rank f` —\n21\t  `finrank_range_adjoint`), and\n22\t* Krein-self-adjoint carriers (`D₋ = J₊ ∘ D₊† ∘ J₋` with fundamental symmetries `J±`\n23\t  invertible, since conjugation by equivalences preserves rank —\n24\t  `finrank_range_conj_equiv`) — the case of the null-edge carrier `D^# = D`.\n25\t\n26\t## Physics readout (finite identity, honestly scoped)\n27\t\n28\t**No potential `φ` and no transport `∇` can change the chiral index**: mass generation\n29\tcan gap chiral modes only in `±` pairs; a surplus `|dim M₊ − dim M₋|` of massless chiral\n30\tmodes is topologically protected. In the null-edge program this is the complement of the\n31\tmass thesis: mass = obstruction to null transport; masslessness (of the protected\n32\tsurplus) = index theory of the complex. This is the finite-dimensional shadow of the\n33\tAtiyah-Singer/McKean-Singer index theorem — `str(e^{−tD²}) = ind(D)` — whose finite\n34\tcontent is exactly the pairing of nonzero-mass² modes across chirality; the heat-kernel\n35\tsupertrace version (matrix exponential) is deliberately NOT formalized here, only the\n36\t`t → ∞` combinatorial core, which is the part with kernel-checkable content on a finite\n37\tcomplex.\n38\t\n39\tClaim labels: everything here is a **finite identity** on finite-dimensional modules.\n40\tNo continuum limit, no spectral claim, no statement about which physical carrier\n41\trealizes which `dim M₊ − dim M₋`.\n42\t\n43\tProvenance: McKean-Singer (1967) \"Curvature and the eigenvalues of the Laplacian\";\n44\tstandard folklore finite version (e.g. Getzler-Berline-Vergne, ch. 1 exercises);\n45\tclean-room formalization from rank-nullity, no external code.\n46\t-/\n47\t\n48\topen scoped InnerProductSpace\n49\t\n50\tnamespace PhysicsSM.Draft.NullEdge.Carrier\n51\t\n52\tsection Core\n53\t\n54\tvariable {K : Type*} [DivisionRing K] {Mp Mm : Type*}\n55\t  [AddCommGroup Mp] [Module K Mp] [AddCommGroup Mm] [Module K Mm]\n56\t\n57\t/-- The **chiral index** of an odd (Weyl block) operator pair `D₊ : M₊ → M₋`,\n58\t`D₋ : M₋ → M₊`: massless positive-chirality modes minus massless negative-chirality\n59\tmodes, `dim ker D₊ − dim ker D₋ : ℤ`. -/\n60\tnoncomputable def chiralIndex (Dp : Mp →ₗ[K] Mm) (Dm : Mm →ₗ[K] Mp) : ℤ :=\n61\t  (Module.finrank K (LinearMap.ker Dp) : ℤ) - (Module.finrank K (LinearMap.ker Dm) : ℤ)\n62\t\n63\t/-- **Finite McKean-Singer, combinatorial core.** If the two chiral blocks have equal\n64\trank, the chiral index equals the graded dimension `dim M₊ − dim M₋` — a number fixed by\n65\tthe complex alone. Pure rank-nullity. -/\n66\ttheorem chiralIndex_eq_graded_dimension\n67\t    [FiniteDimensional K Mp] [FiniteDimensional K Mm]\n68\t    (Dp : Mp →ₗ[K] Mm) (Dm : Mm →ₗ[K] Mp)\n69\t    (hrank : Module.finrank K (LinearMap.range Dp)\n70\t      = Module.finrank K (LinearMap.range Dm)) :\n71\t    chiralIndex Dp Dm\n72\t      = (Module.finrank K Mp : ℤ) - (Module.finrank K Mm : ℤ) := by\n73\t  have h1 := LinearMap.finrank_range_add_finrank_ker Dp\n74\t  have h2 := LinearMap.finrank_range_add_finrank_ker Dm\n75\t  rw [chiralIndex]\n76\t  omega\n77\t\n78\t/-- **Index protection.** Two carriers with rank-symmetric blocks — ANY two potentials,\n79\tANY two transports — have the same chiral index: mass generation cannot lift protected\n80\tchiral zero modes. -/\n81\ttheorem chiralIndex_protected\n82\t    [FiniteDimensional K Mp] [FiniteDimensional K Mm]\n83\t    (Dp Dp' : Mp →ₗ[K] Mm) (Dm Dm' : Mm →ₗ[K] Mp)\n84\t    (hrank : Module.finrank K (LinearMap.range Dp)\n85\t      = Module.finrank K (LinearMap.range Dm))\n86\t    (hrank' : Module.finrank K (LinearMap.range Dp')\n87\t      = Module.finrank K (LinearMap.range Dm')) :\n88\t    chiralIndex Dp Dm = chiralIndex Dp' Dm' := by\n89\t  rw [chiralIndex_eq_graded_dimension Dp Dm hrank,\n90\t    chiralIndex_eq_graded_dimension Dp' Dm' hrank']\n91\t\n92\tend Core\n93\t\n94\tsection InnerProduct\n95\t\n96\tvariable {𝕜 : Type*} [RCLike 𝕜] {E F : Type*}\n97\t  [NormedAddCommGroup E] [InnerProductSpace 𝕜 E]\n98\t  [NormedAddCommGroup F] [InnerProductSpace 𝕜 F]\n99\t  [FiniteDimensional 𝕜 E] [FiniteDimensional 𝕜 F]\n100\t\n101\t/-- The kernel of the adjoint is the orthogonal complement of the range (finite\n102\tdimensions). -/\n103\ttheorem ker_adjoint_eq_orthogonal_range (f : E →ₗ[𝕜] F) :\n104\t    LinearMap.ker (LinearMap.adjoint f) = (LinearMap.range f)ᗮ := by\n105\t  ext x\n106\t  rw [LinearMap.mem_ker, Submodule.mem_orthogonal]\n107\t  constructor\n108\t  · intro h u hu\n109\t    obtain ⟨y, rfl⟩ := hu\n110\t    rw [← LinearMap.adjoint_inner_right, h, inner_zero_right]\n111\t  · intro h\n112\t    have h0 : (inner 𝕜 (f (LinearMap.adjoint f x)) x : 𝕜) = 0 :=\n113\t      h _ ⟨LinearMap.adjoint f x, rfl⟩\n114\t    have h1 : (inner 𝕜 (LinearMap.adjoint f x) (LinearMap.adjoint f x) : 𝕜) = 0 := by\n115\t      rw [LinearMap.adjoint_inner_right]\n116\t      exact h0\n117\t    exact inner_self_eq_zero.mp h1\n118\t\n119\t/-- The adjoint has the same rank as the map (finite dimensions): rank-nullity on the\n120\tadjoint plus `dim range + dim rangeᗮ = dim F`. -/\n121\ttheorem finrank_range_adjoint (f : E →ₗ[𝕜] F) :\n122\t    Module.finrank 𝕜 (LinearMap.range (LinearMap.adjoint f))\n123\t      = Module.finrank 𝕜 (LinearMap.range f) := by\n124\t  have h1 := LinearMap.finrank_range_add_finrank_ker (LinearMap.adjoint f)\n125\t  have h2 := Submodule.finrank_add_finrank_orthogonal (LinearMap.range f)\n126\t  rw [ker_adjoint_eq_orthogonal_range] at h1\n127\t  omega\n128\t\n129\tomit [FiniteDimensional 𝕜 E] [FiniteDimensional 𝕜 F] in\n130\t/-- Conjugating by linear equivalences (e.g. fundamental symmetries `J`) preserves\n131\trank. -/\n132\ttheorem finrank_range_conj_equiv (g : F →ₗ[𝕜] E) (Jpre : F ≃ₗ[𝕜] F) (Jpost : E ≃ₗ[𝕜] E) :\n133\t    Module.finrank 𝕜\n134\t        (LinearMap.range (Jpost.toLinearMap ∘ₗ g ∘ₗ Jpre.toLinearMap))\n135\t      = Module.finrank 𝕜 (LinearMap.range g) := by\n136\t  rw [LinearMap.range_comp, LinearMap.range_comp, LinearEquiv.range,\n137\t    Submodule.map_top, LinearEquiv.finrank_map_eq]\n138\t\n139\t/-- **Index protection, Hilbert-self-adjoint form.** For the adjoint pair\n140\t`(D₊, D₊†)` — the block form of a Hilbert-self-adjoint odd carrier — the chiral index\n141\tIS the graded dimension, with no rank hypothesis: it is independent of `D₊`\n142\taltogether. -/\n143\ttheorem chiralIndex_adjoint_pair (Dp : E →ₗ[𝕜] F) :\n144\t    chiralIndex Dp (LinearMap.adjoint Dp)\n145\t      = (Module.finrank 𝕜 E : ℤ) - (Module.finrank 𝕜 F : ℤ) :=\n146\t  chiralIndex_eq_graded_dimension Dp (LinearMap.adjoint Dp)\n147\t    (finrank_range_adjoint Dp).symm\n148\t\n149\t/-- **Index protection, Krein-self-adjoint form.** For the Krein-adjoint pair\n150\t`(D₊, J_E ∘ D₊† ∘ J_F)` with invertible fundamental symmetries `J` — the block form of\n151\ta KREIN-self-adjoint odd carrier, the null-edge case — the chiral index is again the\n152\tgraded dimension, independent of `D₊`, `φ`, `∇`, and of the `J`s. -/\n153\ttheorem chiralIndex_krein_pair (Dp : E →ₗ[𝕜] F) (JE : E ≃ₗ[𝕜] E) (JF : F ≃ₗ[𝕜] F) :\n154\t    chiralIndex Dp (JE.toLinearMap ∘ₗ LinearMap.adjoint Dp ∘ₗ JF.toLinearMap)\n155\t      = (Module.finrank 𝕜 E : ℤ) - (Module.finrank 𝕜 F : ℤ) :=\n156\t  chiralIndex_eq_graded_dimension Dp _\n157\t    (by rw [finrank_range_conj_equiv, finrank_range_adjoint])\n158\t\n159\tend InnerProduct\n160\t\n161\tsection ProtectedMode\n162\t\n163\tvariable {𝕜 : Type*} [RCLike 𝕜] {E F : Type*}\n164\t  [NormedAddCommGroup E] [InnerProductSpace 𝕜 E]\n165\t  [NormedAddCommGroup F] [InnerProductSpace 𝕜 F]\n166\t  [FiniteDimensional 𝕜 E] [FiniteDimensional 𝕜 F]\n167\t\n168\t/-- **A protected massless chiral mode, exhibited.**  On a graded complex with\n169\tunbalanced chirality (`dim M₋ < dim M₊`), EVERY carrier block `D₊ : M₊ → M₋` — any\n170\ttransport, any potential — annihilates some nonzero positive-chirality state: a\n171\tmassless mode no mass generation can remove (only changing the complex can).\n172\t\n173\tHonesty note: this exhibit-form is elementary rank-nullity and does not need the index\n174\tmachinery; the index content proper (`chiralIndex_adjoint_pair`,\n175\t`chiralIndex_krein_pair`) is that the surplus count is EXACT and deformation-invariant,\n176\tnot merely nonzero. -/\n177\ttheorem exists_protected_massless_mode\n178\t    (hdim : Module.finrank 𝕜 F < Module.finrank 𝕜 E) (Dp : E →ₗ[𝕜] F) :\n179\t    ∃ ψ : E, ψ ≠ 0 ∧ Dp ψ = 0 := by\n180\t  have h1 := LinearMap.finrank_range_add_finrank_ker Dp\n181\t  have h2 : Module.finrank 𝕜 (LinearMap.range Dp) ≤ Module.finrank 𝕜 F :=\n182\t    Submodule.finrank_le _\n183\t  have hker : 0 < Module.finrank 𝕜 (LinearMap.ker Dp) := by omega\n184\t  have hne : LinearMap.ker Dp ≠ ⊥ := by\n185\t    intro hbot\n186\t    rw [hbot, finrank_bot] at hker\n187\t    exact lt_irrefl 0 hker\n188\t  obtain ⟨ψ, hmem, hpsine⟩ := (Submodule.ne_bot_iff _).mp hne\n189\t  exact ⟨ψ, hpsine, hmem⟩\n190\t\n191\tend ProtectedMode\n192\t\n193\tsection ChiralWitness\n194\t\n195\t/-! ## The chiral pole: a concrete unbalanced complex\n196\t\n197\tThe kappa=2 Pontryagin witness (`CarrierPontryaginWitness.lean`) has BALANCED grading\n198\t(2,2), index 0 — nothing protected, everything gappable, and indeed its mass form is\n199\tstrictly positive. Here is the opposite pole: chirality grading `(2,1)` on `ℂ² ⊕ ℂ¹`,\n200\twhere the index is `1` for EVERY Hilbert-self-adjoint carrier and a massless\n201\tpositive-chirality mode is forced — the \"massless neutrino\" of the toy family. The two\n202\twitnesses bracket the program: grading balance decides whether mass generation can be\n203\tcomplete. -/\n204\t\n205\t/-- On the `(2,1)`-graded complex the chiral index of every adjoint carrier pair is\n206\t`1` — one protected massless chiral mode, whatever the dynamics. -/\n207\ttheorem chiralWitness_index_one\n208\t    (Dp : EuclideanSpace ℂ (Fin 2) →ₗ[ℂ] EuclideanSpace ℂ (Fin 1)) :\n209\t    chiralIndex Dp (LinearMap.adjoint Dp) = 1 := by\n210\t  rw [chiralIndex_adjoint_pair]\n211\t  simp\n212\t\n213\t/-- On the `(2,1)`-graded complex EVERY carrier block annihilates a nonzero\n214\tpositive-chirality state: the forced massless mode, exhibited concretely. -/\n215\ttheorem chiralWitness_forced_massless_mode\n216\t    (Dp : EuclideanSpace ℂ (Fin 2) →ₗ[ℂ] EuclideanSpace ℂ (Fin 1)) :\n217\t    ∃ ψ : EuclideanSpace ℂ (Fin 2), ψ ≠ 0 ∧ Dp ψ = 0 :=\n218\t  exists_protected_massless_mode (by simp) Dp\n219\t\n220\tend ChiralWitness\n221\t\n222\tend PhysicsSM.Draft.NullEdge.Carrier\n223\t"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"e6c6e3b0-0265-4770-b1a6-d84ed8d06188","uuid":"d80312e0-6450-4d5f-8c5a-cea1f6bbcc87","timestamp":"2026-07-08T07:30:51.177Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"C:\\Projects\\StandardModel\\PhysicsSM\\Draft\\NullEdge\\Carrier\\CarrierIndexProtection.lean","content":"import Mathlib\n\n/-!\n# Index protection of massless chiral modes: the finite McKean-Singer mechanism\n\nThe mass thesis explains what pushes states OFF the light cone; this file formalizes the\nconverse jewel — what must STAY on it. For a chirality-odd carrier written in Weyl block\nform `D = [[0, D₋], [D₊, 0]]` on `M = M₊ ⊕ M₋` (the `Γ = ±1` sectors), the **chiral\nindex**\n\n>  `ind(D) := dim ker D₊ − dim ker D₋`\n\ncounts massless positive-chirality modes minus massless negative-chirality modes (for a\ncarrier, `ker D₊ = ker D ∩ M₊` is precisely the massless chiral sector). The finite\nMcKean-Singer mechanism is pure rank-nullity: whenever the two blocks have equal rank,\n\n>  `ind(D) = dim M₊ − dim M₋`,\n\na number fixed by the complex alone. The rank hypothesis is **automatic** for\n* Hilbert-self-adjoint carriers (`D₋ = D₊†`, since `rank f† = rank f` —\n  `finrank_range_adjoint`), and\n* Krein-self-adjoint carriers (`D₋ = J₊ ∘ D₊† ∘ J₋` with fundamental symmetries `J±`\n  invertible, since conjugation by equivalences preserves rank —\n  `finrank_range_conj_equiv`) — the case of the null-edge carrier `D^# = D`.\n\n## Physics readout (finite identity, honestly scoped)\n\n**No potential `φ` and no transport `∇` can change the chiral index**: mass generation\ncan gap chiral modes only in `±` pairs; a surplus `|dim M₊ − dim M₋|` of massless chiral\nmodes is topologically protected. In the null-edge program this is the complement of the\nmass thesis: mass = obstruction to null transport; masslessness (of the protected\nsurplus) = index theory of the complex. This is the finite-dimensional shadow of the\nAtiyah-Singer/McKean-Singer index theorem — `str(e^{−tD²}) = ind(D)` — whose finite\ncontent is exactly the pairing of nonzero-mass² modes across chirality; the heat-kernel\nsupertrace version (matrix exponential) is deliberately NOT formalized here, only the\n`t → ∞` combinatorial core, which is the part with kernel-checkable content on a finite\ncomplex.\n\nClaim labels: everything here is a **finite identity** on finite-dimensional modules.\nNo continuum limit, no spectral claim, no statement about which physical carrier\nrealizes which `dim M₊ − dim M₋`.\n\nProvenance: McKean-Singer (1967) \"Curvature and the eigenvalues of the Laplacian\";\nstandard folklore finite version (e.g. Getzler-Berline-Vergne, ch. 1 exercises);\nclean-room formalization from rank-nullity, no external code.\n-/\n\nopen scoped InnerProductSpace\n\nnamespace PhysicsSM.Draft.NullEdge.Carrier\n\nsection Core\n\nvariable {K : Type*} [DivisionRing K] {Mp Mm : Type*}\n  [AddCommGroup Mp] [Module K Mp] [AddCommGroup Mm] [Module K Mm]\n\n/-- The **chiral index** of an odd (Weyl block) operator pair `D₊ : M₊ → M₋`,\n`D₋ : M₋ → M₊`: massless positive-chirality modes minus massless negative-chirality\nmodes, `dim ker D₊ − dim ker D₋ : ℤ`. -/\nnoncomputable def chiralIndex (Dp : Mp →ₗ[K] Mm) (Dm : Mm →ₗ[K] Mp) : ℤ :=\n  (Module.finrank K (LinearMap.ker Dp) : ℤ) - (Module.finrank K (LinearMap.ker Dm) : ℤ)\n\n/-- **Finite McKean-Singer, combinatorial core.** If the two chiral blocks have equal\nrank, the chiral index equals the graded dimension `dim M₊ − dim M₋` — a number fixed by\nthe complex alone. Pure rank-nullity. -/\ntheorem chiralIndex_eq_graded_dimension\n    [FiniteDimensional K Mp] [FiniteDimensional K Mm]\n    (Dp : Mp →ₗ[K] Mm) (Dm : Mm →ₗ[K] Mp)\n    (hrank : Module.finrank K (LinearMap.range Dp)\n      = Module.finrank K (LinearMap.range Dm)) :\n    chiralIndex Dp Dm\n      = (Module.finrank K Mp : ℤ) - (Module.finrank K Mm : ℤ) := by\n  have h1 := LinearMap.finrank_range_add_finrank_ker Dp\n  have h2 := LinearMap.finrank_range_add_finrank_ker Dm\n  rw [chiralIndex]\n  omega\n\n/-- **Index protection.** Two carriers with rank-symmetric blocks — ANY two potentials,\nANY two transports — have the same chiral index: mass generation cannot lift protected\nchiral zero modes. -/\ntheorem chiralIndex_protected\n    [FiniteDimensional K Mp] [FiniteDimensional K Mm]\n    (Dp Dp' : Mp →ₗ[K] Mm) (Dm Dm' : Mm →ₗ[K] Mp)\n    (hrank : Module.finrank K (LinearMap.range Dp)\n      = Module.finrank K (LinearMap.range Dm))\n    (hrank' : Module.finrank K (LinearMap.range Dp')\n      = Module.finrank K (LinearMap.range Dm')) :\n    chiralIndex Dp Dm = chiralIndex Dp' Dm' := by\n  rw [chiralIndex_eq_graded_dimension Dp Dm hrank,\n    chiralIndex_eq_graded_dimension Dp' Dm' hrank']\n\nend Core\n\nsection InnerProduct\n\nvariable {𝕜 : Type*} [RCLike 𝕜] {E F : Type*}\n  [NormedAddCommGroup E] [InnerProductSpace 𝕜 E]\n  [NormedAddCommGroup F] [InnerProductSpace 𝕜 F]\n  [FiniteDimensional 𝕜 E] [FiniteDimensional 𝕜 F]\n\n/-- The kernel of the adjoint is the orthogonal complement of the range (finite\ndimensions). -/\ntheorem ker_adjoint_eq_orthogonal_range (f : E →ₗ[𝕜] F) :\n    LinearMap.ker (LinearMap.adjoint f) = (LinearMap.range f)ᗮ := by\n  ext x\n  rw [LinearMap.mem_ker, Submodule.mem_orthogonal]\n  constructor\n  · intro h u hu\n    obtain ⟨y, rfl⟩ := hu\n    rw [← LinearMap.adjoint_inner_right, h, inner_zero_right]\n  · intro h\n    have h0 : (inner 𝕜 (f (LinearMap.adjoint f x)) x : 𝕜) = 0 :=\n      h _ ⟨LinearMap.adjoint f x, rfl⟩\n    have h1 : (inner 𝕜 (LinearMap.adjoint f x) (LinearMap.adjoint f x) : 𝕜) = 0 := by\n      rw [LinearMap.adjoint_inner_right]\n      exact h0\n    exact inner_self_eq_zero.mp h1\n\n/-- The adjoint has the same rank as the map (finite dimensions): rank-nullity on the\nadjoint plus `dim range + dim rangeᗮ = dim F`. -/\ntheorem finrank_range_adjoint (f : E →ₗ[𝕜] F) :\n    Module.finrank 𝕜 (LinearMap.range (LinearMap.adjoint f))\n      = Module.finrank 𝕜 (LinearMap.range f) := by\n  have h1 := LinearMap.finrank_range_add_finrank_ker (LinearMap.adjoint f)\n  have h2 := Submodule.finrank_add_finrank_orthogonal (LinearMap.range f)\n  rw [ker_adjoint_eq_orthogonal_range] at h1\n  omega\n\nomit [FiniteDimensional 𝕜 E] [FiniteDimensional 𝕜 F] in\n/-- Conjugating by linear equivalences (e.g. fundamental symmetries `J`) preserves\nrank. -/\ntheorem finrank_range_conj_equiv (g : F →ₗ[𝕜] E) (Jpre : F ≃ₗ[𝕜] F) (Jpost : E ≃ₗ[𝕜] E) :\n    Module.finrank 𝕜\n        (LinearMap.range (Jpost.toLinearMap ∘ₗ g ∘ₗ Jpre.toLinearMap))\n      = Module.finrank 𝕜 (LinearMap.range g) := by\n  rw [LinearMap.range_comp, LinearMap.range_comp, LinearEquiv.range,\n    Submodule.map_top, LinearEquiv.finrank_map_eq]\n\n/-- **Index protection, Hilbert-self-adjoint form.** For the adjoint pair\n`(D₊, D₊†)` — the block form of a Hilbert-self-adjoint odd carrier — the chiral index\nIS the graded dimension, with no rank hypothesis: it is independent of `D₊`\naltogether. -/\ntheorem chiralIndex_adjoint_pair (Dp : E →ₗ[𝕜] F) :\n    chiralIndex Dp (LinearMap.adjoint Dp)\n      = (Module.finrank 𝕜 E : ℤ) - (Module.finrank 𝕜 F : ℤ) :=\n  chiralIndex_eq_graded_dimension Dp (LinearMap.adjoint Dp)\n    (finrank_range_adjoint Dp).symm\n\n/-- **Index protection, Krein-self-adjoint form.** For the Krein-adjoint pair\n`(D₊, J_E ∘ D₊† ∘ J_F)` with invertible fundamental symmetries `J` — the block form of\na KREIN-self-adjoint odd carrier, the null-edge case — the chiral index is again the\ngraded dimension, independent of `D₊`, `φ`, `∇`, and of the `J`s. -/\ntheorem chiralIndex_krein_pair (Dp : E →ₗ[𝕜] F) (JE : E ≃ₗ[𝕜] E) (JF : F ≃ₗ[𝕜] F) :\n    chiralIndex Dp (JE.toLinearMap ∘ₗ LinearMap.adjoint Dp ∘ₗ JF.toLinearMap)\n      = (Module.finrank 𝕜 E : ℤ) - (Module.finrank 𝕜 F : ℤ) :=\n  chiralIndex_eq_graded_dimension Dp _\n    (by rw [finrank_range_conj_equiv, finrank_range_adjoint])\n\nend InnerProduct\n\nsection ProtectedMode\n\nvariable {𝕜 : Type*} [RCLike 𝕜] {E F : Type*}\n  [NormedAddCommGroup E] [InnerProductSpace 𝕜 E]\n  [NormedAddCommGroup F] [InnerProductSpace 𝕜 F]\n  [FiniteDimensional 𝕜 E] [FiniteDimensional 𝕜 F]\n\n/-- **A protected massless chiral mode, exhibited.**  On a graded complex with\nunbalanced chirality (`dim M₋ < dim M₊`), EVERY carrier block `D₊ : M₊ → M₋` — any\ntransport, any potential — annihilates some nonzero positive-chirality state: a\nmassless mode no mass generation can remove (only changing the complex can).\n\nHonesty note: this exhibit-form is elementary rank-nullity and does not need the index\nmachinery; the index content proper (`chiralIndex_adjoint_pair`,\n`chiralIndex_krein_pair`) is that the surplus count is EXACT and deformation-invariant,\nnot merely nonzero. -/\ntheorem exists_protected_massless_mode\n    (hdim : Module.finrank 𝕜 F < Module.finrank 𝕜 E) (Dp : E →ₗ[𝕜] F) :\n    ∃ ψ : E, ψ ≠ 0 ∧ Dp ψ = 0 := by\n  have h1 := LinearMap.finrank_range_add_finrank_ker Dp\n  have h2 : Module.finrank 𝕜 (LinearMap.range Dp) ≤ Module.finrank 𝕜 F :=\n    Submodule.finrank_le _\n  have hker : 0 < Module.finrank 𝕜 (LinearMap.ker Dp) := by omega\n  have hne : LinearMap.ker Dp ≠ ⊥ := by\n    intro hbot\n    rw [hbot, finrank_bot] at hker\n    exact lt_irrefl 0 hker\n  obtain ⟨ψ, hmem, hpsine⟩ := (Submodule.ne_bot_iff _).mp hne\n  exact ⟨ψ, hpsine, hmem⟩\n\nend ProtectedMode\n\nsection ChiralWitness\n\n/-! ## The chiral pole: a concrete unbalanced complex\n\nThe kappa=2 Pontryagin witness (`CarrierPontryaginWitness.lean`) has BALANCED grading\n(2,2), index 0 — nothing protected, everything gappable, and indeed its mass form is\nstrictly positive. Here is the opposite pole: chirality grading `(2,1)` on `ℂ² ⊕ ℂ¹`,\nwhere the index is `1` for EVERY Hilbert-self-adjoint carrier and a massless\npositive-chirality mode is forced — the \"massless neutrino\" of the toy family. The two\nwitnesses bracket the program: grading balance decides whether mass generation can be\ncomplete. -/\n\n/-- On the `(2,1)`-graded complex the chiral index of every adjoint carrier pair is\n`1` — one protected massless chiral mode, whatever the dynamics. -/\ntheorem chiralWitness_index_one\n    (Dp : EuclideanSpace ℂ (Fin 2) →ₗ[ℂ] EuclideanSpace ℂ (Fin 1)) :\n    chiralIndex Dp (LinearMap.adjoint Dp) = 1 := by\n  rw [chiralIndex_adjoint_pair]\n  simp\n\n/-- On the `(2,1)`-graded complex EVERY carrier block annihilates a nonzero\npositive-chirality state: the forced massless mode, exhibited concretely. -/\ntheorem chiralWitness_forced_massless_mode\n    (Dp : EuclideanSpace ℂ (Fin 2) →ₗ[ℂ] EuclideanSpace ℂ (Fin 1)) :\n    ∃ ψ : EuclideanSpace ℂ (Fin 2), ψ ≠ 0 ∧ Dp ψ = 0 :=\n  exists_protected_massless_mode (by simp) Dp\n\nend ChiralWitness\n\nend PhysicsSM.Draft.NullEdge.Carrier\n","numLines":223,"startLine":1,"totalLines":223}}}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01TVjqDbXtgzfPHUSEiGHAby","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t/-\n2\t# Chiral-symmetry forcing of protected transfer eigenvalues (T1)\n3\t\n4\tDRAFT (kernel-clean; no `s o r r y`). The K6 target of the overnight\n5\tall-mass run (2026-07-08), redirected by the determinant-parity probe\n6\t`Scripts/oracle/p1_zeromode_symmetry_invariant.py`.\n7\t\n8\t## What the probe decided (numeric oracle; guides this statement)\n9\t\n10\tThe protected unit eigenvalue of the decorated-cycle transfer `W` is NOT\n11\tforced by cyclic symmetry - abstract winding-1 symmetric data is UNPINNED\n12\t(min|ev - 1| ~ 0.2-0.45), and equivariant-but-unpinned cases are common.\n13\tIt is forced by a CHIRAL symmetry: an involution `Gamma` (`Gamma^2 = 1`)\n14\twith `Gamma W Gamma = W^dagger`. Numerically this `Gamma` is the\n15\torientation-swap, i.e. the edge-orientation-reversal grading - the SAME\n16\tgrading that gives the exact Ginsparg-Wilson structure\n17\t(`GWRetardedTransfer.lean`). It is present exactly for the even-V\n18\thalf-winding (alternating-phase) decoration and there pins BOTH `+1` and\n19\t`-1` in `spec(W)` for every hop amplitude `|t|` (probe: V = 4, 6, 8).\n20\t\n21\t## What this module proves (the honest kernel core)\n22\t\n23\tFor a unitary `W` carrying such a chiral involution:\n24\t\n25\t* `chiral_det_conj`: `det W` is real (`conj (det W) = det W`);\n26\t* `chiral_det_sq_one`: `(det W)^2 = 1`;\n27\t* `chiral_det_eq_pm_one`: hence `det W = 1` or `det W = -1`.\n28\t\n29\tSince `det W = (-1)^{mult(-1)}` up to conjugate-pair factors, the sign of\n30\t`det W` fixes the PARITY of the `-1`-eigenvalue multiplicity - the honest\n31\tcore of the determinant-parity mechanism (SevenChallenges memo finding 8).\n32\t\n33\tCORRECTION (Fable call-02, 2026-07-08): the full `|t|`-independent DOUBLE\n34\tpinning (both `+-1` present at even dimension) is NOT forced by a global\n35\twinding invariant - Fable measured the global Asboth-Obuse-type index to\n36\tVANISH for the half-winding decoration (`tr Gamma = 0`, `tr(Gamma W) = 0`,\n37\t`nu_0 = nu_pi = 0`), while `mult(+1) = mult(-1) = 2`. The correct mechanism\n38\tis an EQUIVARIANT REFLECTION-SECTORED index: `W` commutes with a reflection\n39\t`R` (leg-reversal composed with orientation swap, `R^2 = 1`, `[R, Gamma]\n40\t= 0`), and the two `R`-sectors carry OPPOSITE chiral indices `(+1,+1)` and\n41\t`(-1,-1)` that cancel globally but each pin one `+-1` mode. The sector\n42\tindex is `nu = +- (1/4) tr(Gamma R)`, a Lefschetz fixed-point count (`=\n43\t#fixed legs`) that does NOT involve `W` - which is why the pinning is\n44\t`|t|`-independent. Full analysis and the `M`-target sectored-pinning\n45\ttheorem: Fable call-02 log + the run's C4 note; the abstract engine here\n46\t(`det = +-1`) stands.\n47\t\n48\t## Claim boundary\n49\t\n50\tFinite matrix algebra, no spectral theorem. `det = +-1` is proved; the\n51\teigenvalue-multiplicity reading is stated in prose, not in a theorem.\n52\tNo continuum, no physical-mode claim beyond the finite determinant fact.\n53\t\n54\t## Provenance\n55\t\n56\tMechanism identified by the overnight K6 probe (2026-07-08); the chiral\n57\t`Gamma U Gamma = U^dagger` structure and its `0/pi`-mode consequences are\n58\tstandard for chiral-symmetric quantum walks (Asboth-Obuse 2013,\n59\tarXiv:1303.1199, in the paper graph) - [import]/[comp]. The edge-reversal\n60\tidentification is this program's (`GWRetardedTransfer.lean`) - [orig].\n61\t-/\n62\t\n63\timport Mathlib\n64\t\n65\tnamespace PhysicsSM.Draft.NullEdge.Carrier.ChiralZeroModeParity\n66\t\n67\topen Matrix\n68\t\n69\tvariable {n : Type*} [Fintype n] [DecidableEq n]\n70\t\n71\t/-- A **chiral involution** for `W`: a self-inverse `Gamma` conjugating `W`\n72\tto its adjoint. For unitary `W` (`Wᴴ * W = 1`) this says `Gamma` conjugates\n73\t`W` to `W⁻¹` - the reciprocal-pairing (chiral) symmetry. -/\n74\tstructure ChiralInvolution (W Gamma : Matrix n n ℂ) : Prop where\n75\t  invol : Gamma * Gamma = 1\n76\t  chiral : Gamma * W * Gamma = Wᴴ\n77\t\n78\t/-- Under a chiral involution, `det Gamma` squares to one. -/\n79\ttheorem chiral_detGamma_sq {W Gamma : Matrix n n ℂ}\n80\t    (h : ChiralInvolution W Gamma) : Gamma.det * Gamma.det = 1 := by\n81\t  rw [← Matrix.det_mul, h.invol, Matrix.det_one]\n82\t\n83\t/-- **`det W` is real** under a chiral involution: `conj (det W) = det W`.\n84\t(Taking `det` of `Gamma W Gamma = Wᴴ` and using `det Gamma ^2 = 1`.) -/\n85\ttheorem chiral_det_conj {W Gamma : Matrix n n ℂ}\n86\t    (h : ChiralInvolution W Gamma) :\n87\t    (starRingEnd ℂ) W.det = W.det := by\n88\t  have hdet : Gamma.det * W.det * Gamma.det = (Wᴴ).det := by\n89\t    rw [← Matrix.det_mul, ← Matrix.det_mul, h.chiral]\n90\t  rw [Matrix.det_conjTranspose] at hdet\n91\t  -- LHS = det W * (det Gamma)^2 = det W\n92\t  have hcomm : Gamma.det * W.det * Gamma.det\n93\t      = W.det * (Gamma.det * Gamma.det) := by ring\n94\t  rw [hcomm, chiral_detGamma_sq h, mul_one] at hdet\n95\t  exact hdet.symm\n96\t\n97\t/-- Under a chiral involution with `W` unitary, `(det W)^2 = 1`. -/\n98\ttheorem chiral_det_sq_one {W Gamma : Matrix n n ℂ}\n99\t    (h : ChiralInvolution W Gamma) (hU : Wᴴ * W = 1) :\n100\t    W.det * W.det = 1 := by\n101\t  -- |det W|^2 = 1 from unitarity, and det W real from the chiral symmetry\n102\t  have hmod : (starRingEnd ℂ) W.det * W.det = 1 := by\n103\t    have : (Wᴴ * W).det = 1 := by rw [hU, Matrix.det_one]\n104\t    rwa [Matrix.det_mul, Matrix.det_conjTranspose] at this\n105\t  rw [chiral_det_conj h] at hmod\n106\t  exact hmod\n107\t\n108\t/-- **The chiral determinant dichotomy (T1 core).** A unitary `W` carrying\n109\ta chiral involution has `det W = 1` or `det W = -1`. The sign fixes the\n110\tparity of the `-1`-eigenvalue multiplicity: the honest kernel core of the\n111\tdeterminant-parity forcing of protected `+-1` transfer modes. -/\n112\ttheorem chiral_det_eq_pm_one {W Gamma : Matrix n n ℂ}\n113\t    (h : ChiralInvolution W Gamma) (hU : Wᴴ * W = 1) :\n114\t    W.det = 1 ∨ W.det = -1 := by\n115\t  have hsq : W.det * W.det = 1 := chiral_det_sq_one h hU\n116\t  have : (W.det - 1) * (W.det + 1) = 0 := by\n117\t    have : (W.det - 1) * (W.det + 1) = W.det * W.det - 1 := by ring\n118\t    rw [this, hsq, sub_self]\n119\t  rcases mul_eq_zero.mp this with h1 | h2\n120\t  · exact Or.inl (by linear_combination h1)\n121\t  · exact Or.inr (by linear_combination h2)\n122\t\n123\tend PhysicsSM.Draft.NullEdge.Carrier.ChiralZeroModeParity\n124\t"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"e6c6e3b0-0265-4770-b1a6-d84ed8d06188","uuid":"516564d7-da24-4481-952a-b3bb2f2e81b2","timestamp":"2026-07-08T07:30:58.028Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"C:\\Projects\\StandardModel\\PhysicsSM\\Draft\\NullEdge\\Carrier\\ChiralZeroModeParity.lean","content":"/-\n# Chiral-symmetry forcing of protected transfer eigenvalues (T1)\n\nDRAFT (kernel-clean; no `s o r r y`). The K6 target of the overnight\nall-mass run (2026-07-08), redirected by the determinant-parity probe\n`Scripts/oracle/p1_zeromode_symmetry_invariant.py`.\n\n## What the probe decided (numeric oracle; guides this statement)\n\nThe protected unit eigenvalue of the decorated-cycle transfer `W` is NOT\nforced by cyclic symmetry - abstract winding-1 symmetric data is UNPINNED\n(min|ev - 1| ~ 0.2-0.45), and equivariant-but-unpinned cases are common.\nIt is forced by a CHIRAL symmetry: an involution `Gamma` (`Gamma^2 = 1`)\nwith `Gamma W Gamma = W^dagger`. Numerically this `Gamma` is the\norientation-swap, i.e. the edge-orientation-reversal grading - the SAME\ngrading that gives the exact Ginsparg-Wilson structure\n(`GWRetardedTransfer.lean`). It is present exactly for the even-V\nhalf-winding (alternating-phase) decoration and there pins BOTH `+1` and\n`-1` in `spec(W)` for every hop amplitude `|t|` (probe: V = 4, 6, 8).\n\n## What this module proves (the honest kernel core)\n\nFor a unitary `W` carrying such a chiral involution:\n\n* `chiral_det_conj`: `det W` is real (`conj (det W) = det W`);\n* `chiral_det_sq_one`: `(det W)^2 = 1`;\n* `chiral_det_eq_pm_one`: hence `det W = 1` or `det W = -1`.\n\nSince `det W = (-1)^{mult(-1)}` up to conjugate-pair factors, the sign of\n`det W` fixes the PARITY of the `-1`-eigenvalue multiplicity - the honest\ncore of the determinant-parity mechanism (SevenChallenges memo finding 8).\n\nCORRECTION (Fable call-02, 2026-07-08): the full `|t|`-independent DOUBLE\npinning (both `+-1` present at even dimension) is NOT forced by a global\nwinding invariant - Fable measured the global Asboth-Obuse-type index to\nVANISH for the half-winding decoration (`tr Gamma = 0`, `tr(Gamma W) = 0`,\n`nu_0 = nu_pi = 0`), while `mult(+1) = mult(-1) = 2`. The correct mechanism\nis an EQUIVARIANT REFLECTION-SECTORED index: `W` commutes with a reflection\n`R` (leg-reversal composed with orientation swap, `R^2 = 1`, `[R, Gamma]\n= 0`), and the two `R`-sectors carry OPPOSITE chiral indices `(+1,+1)` and\n`(-1,-1)` that cancel globally but each pin one `+-1` mode. The sector\nindex is `nu = +- (1/4) tr(Gamma R)`, a Lefschetz fixed-point count (`=\n#fixed legs`) that does NOT involve `W` - which is why the pinning is\n`|t|`-independent. Full analysis and the `M`-target sectored-pinning\ntheorem: Fable call-02 log + the run's C4 note; the abstract engine here\n(`det = +-1`) stands.\n\n## Claim boundary\n\nFinite matrix algebra, no spectral theorem. `det = +-1` is proved; the\neigenvalue-multiplicity reading is stated in prose, not in a theorem.\nNo continuum, no physical-mode claim beyond the finite determinant fact.\n\n## Provenance\n\nMechanism identified by the overnight K6 probe (2026-07-08); the chiral\n`Gamma U Gamma = U^dagger` structure and its `0/pi`-mode consequences are\nstandard for chiral-symmetric quantum walks (Asboth-Obuse 2013,\narXiv:1303.1199, in the paper graph) - [import]/[comp]. The edge-reversal\nidentification is this program's (`GWRetardedTransfer.lean`) - [orig].\n-/\n\nimport Mathlib\n\nnamespace PhysicsSM.Draft.NullEdge.Carrier.ChiralZeroModeParity\n\nopen Matrix\n\nvariable {n : Type*} [Fintype n] [DecidableEq n]\n\n/-- A **chiral involution** for `W`: a self-inverse `Gamma` conjugating `W`\nto its adjoint. For unitary `W` (`Wᴴ * W = 1`) this says `Gamma` conjugates\n`W` to `W⁻¹` - the reciprocal-pairing (chiral) symmetry. -/\nstructure ChiralInvolution (W Gamma : Matrix n n ℂ) : Prop where\n  invol : Gamma * Gamma = 1\n  chiral : Gamma * W * Gamma = Wᴴ\n\n/-- Under a chiral involution, `det Gamma` squares to one. -/\ntheorem chiral_detGamma_sq {W Gamma : Matrix n n ℂ}\n    (h : ChiralInvolution W Gamma) : Gamma.det * Gamma.det = 1 := by\n  rw [← Matrix.det_mul, h.invol, Matrix.det_one]\n\n/-- **`det W` is real** under a chiral involution: `conj (det W) = det W`.\n(Taking `det` of `Gamma W Gamma = Wᴴ` and using `det Gamma ^2 = 1`.) -/\ntheorem chiral_det_conj {W Gamma : Matrix n n ℂ}\n    (h : ChiralInvolution W Gamma) :\n    (starRingEnd ℂ) W.det = W.det := by\n  have hdet : Gamma.det * W.det * Gamma.det = (Wᴴ).det := by\n    rw [← Matrix.det_mul, ← Matrix.det_mul, h.chiral]\n  rw [Matrix.det_conjTranspose] at hdet\n  -- LHS = det W * (det Gamma)^2 = det W\n  have hcomm : Gamma.det * W.det * Gamma.det\n      = W.det * (Gamma.det * Gamma.det) := by ring\n  rw [hcomm, chiral_detGamma_sq h, mul_one] at hdet\n  exact hdet.symm\n\n/-- Under a chiral involution with `W` unitary, `(det W)^2 = 1`. -/\ntheorem chiral_det_sq_one {W Gamma : Matrix n n ℂ}\n    (h : ChiralInvolution W Gamma) (hU : Wᴴ * W = 1) :\n    W.det * W.det = 1 := by\n  -- |det W|^2 = 1 from unitarity, and det W real from the chiral symmetry\n  have hmod : (starRingEnd ℂ) W.det * W.det = 1 := by\n    have : (Wᴴ * W).det = 1 := by rw [hU, Matrix.det_one]\n    rwa [Matrix.det_mul, Matrix.det_conjTranspose] at this\n  rw [chiral_det_conj h] at hmod\n  exact hmod\n\n/-- **The chiral determinant dichotomy (T1 core).** A unitary `W` carrying\na chiral involution has `det W = 1` or `det W = -1`. The sign fixes the\nparity of the `-1`-eigenvalue multiplicity: the honest kernel core of the\ndeterminant-parity forcing of protected `+-1` transfer modes. -/\ntheorem chiral_det_eq_pm_one {W Gamma : Matrix n n ℂ}\n    (h : ChiralInvolution W Gamma) (hU : Wᴴ * W = 1) :\n    W.det = 1 ∨ W.det = -1 := by\n  have hsq : W.det * W.det = 1 := chiral_det_sq_one h hU\n  have : (W.det - 1) * (W.det + 1) = 0 := by\n    have : (W.det - 1) * (W.det + 1) = W.det * W.det - 1 := by ring\n    rw [this, hsq, sub_self]\n  rcases mul_eq_zero.mp this with h1 | h2\n  · exact Or.inl (by linear_combination h1)\n  · exact Or.inr (by linear_combination h2)\n\nend PhysicsSM.Draft.NullEdge.Carrier.ChiralZeroModeParity\n","numLines":124,"startLine":1,"totalLines":124}}}

The overnight all-mass run's K4 -\n5\tthe flagship S6 deliverable, in its Amendment-A3 SIGNED form: the finite\n6\tmass budget of a state splits EXACTLY into aperture / closure / turn\n7\tshares that sum to one, with the shares SIGNED (not asserted positive\n8\tuntil the physical-sector positivity crux closes).\n9\t\n10\t## The two parts\n11\t\n12\t* `signed_budget_sum_one` (abstract): given the guard-pinned Weitzenboeck\n13\t  identity `4 . D² = Q_A + Q_C + 4 . Q_T` and ANY linear functional `ev`\n14\t  (the expectation `<psi, . psi>`) with `ev (D²) != 0`, the shares\n15\t  `b_A = ev Q_A / M²`, `b_C = ev Q_C / M²`, `b_T = 4 ev Q_T / M²`\n16\t  (`M² = 4 ev(D²)`) satisfy `b_A + b_C + b_T = 1`.\n17\t* `witness_budget_*` (vacuity guard): a concrete `2x2` single-edge\n18\t  carrier - `gamma = sigma_x`, `Gamma = sigma_z`, `nabla = 1`, `phi = 1`,\n19\t  `g = 2` - discharges every hypothesis of `carrier_square_assembly`, so\n20\t  the identity holds by construction; with `ev = trace` the closure share\n21\t  is exactly `0` (one edge -> no closure) and the budget is\n22\t  `b_A = 1/2, b_C = 0, b_T = 1/2` - a non-vacuous witness.\n23\t\n24\tThis is the 3-slot `D²` budget (matching the pinned assembly); the 4-slot\n25\t`D^#D` budget with the soldering-gradient share `E` awaits the `D^#D`\n26\tassembly. No positivity is claimed: the shares are signed (Amendment A3);\n27\t`b_C` is the CHROMOMAGNETIC (hyperfine) share, NOT a gluon-energy share\n28\t(Amendment B wording rail).\n29\t\n30\t## Claim boundary\n31\t\n32\t`b_A + b_C + b_T = 1` is proved (abstract + witness). No continuum,\n36\tno hadron-mass claim.\n37\t\n38\t## Provenance\n39\t\n40\tS6 shape from the QCD roadmap (Amendment A3, SevenChallenges finding 7) -\n41\t[comp]; built on the guard-pinned `carrier_square_assembly`\n42\t(`CarrierSquareAssembly.lean`) - [orig].\n43\t-/\n44\t\n45\timport PhysicsSM.Draft.NullEdge.Carrier.CarrierSquareAssembly\n46\t\n47\tnamespace PhysicsSM.Draft.NullEdge.Carrier.MassBudget\n48\t\n49\topen Matrix\n50\t\n51\t/-! ## The abstract signed budget -/\n52\t\n53\t/-- **Signed mass-budget decomposition of unity.** From the Weitzenboeck\n54\tidentity `4 . D² = Q_A + Q_C + 4 . Q_T` and a linear expectation `ev` with\n55\t`ev (D * D) != 0`, the aperture/closure/turn shares of `M² = 4 ev(D²)` sum\n56\tto one. Shares are signed - no positivity assumed. -/\n57\ttheorem signed_budget_sum_one {B : Type*} [Ring B] [Algebra ℂ B]\n58\t    (ev : B →ₗ[ℂ] ℂ) (D QA QC QT : B)\n59\t    (hid : (4 : ℂ) • (D * D) = QA + QC + (4 : ℂ) • QT)\n60\t    (hM : ev (D * D) ≠ 0) :\n61\t    ev QA / (4 * ev (D * D)) + ev QC / (4 * ev (D * D))\n62\t        + (4 * ev QT) / (4 * ev (D * D)) = 1 := by\n63\t  have hev : (4 : ℂ) * ev (D * D) = ev QA + ev QC + 4 * ev QT := by\n64\t    have h := congrArg ev hid\n65\t    rw [map_smul, map_add, map_add, map_smul, smul_eq_mul, smul_eq_mul] at h\n66\t    exact h\n67\t  have hs : (4 : ℂ) * ev (D * D) ≠ 0 := mul_ne_zero (by norm_num) hM\n68\t  rw [← add_div, ← add_div, ← hev, div_self hs]\n69\t\n70\t/-! ## A concrete non-vacuous witness: the 2x2 single-edge carrier -/\n71\t\n72\t/-- Single Clifford generator `sigma_x`. -/\n73\tdef gammaW : Fin 1 → Matrix (Fin 2) (Fin 2) ℂ := fun _ => !![0, 1; 1, 0]\n74\t\n75\t/-- Constant unit transport `nabla = 1`. -/\n76\tdef nablaW : Fin 1 → Matrix (Fin 2) (Fin 2) ℂ := fun _ => 1\n77\t\n78\t/-- Chirality grading `sigma_z`. -/\n79\tdef GammaW : Matrix (Fin 2) (Fin 2) ℂ := !![1, 0; 0, -1]\n80\t\n81\t/-- Unit turn field `phi = 1`. -/\n82\tdef phiW : Matrix (Fin 2) (Fin 2) ℂ := 1\n83\t\n84\t/-- Edge metric `g = 2` (from `{sigma_x, sigma_x} = 2`). -/\n85\tdef gW : Fin 1 → Fin 1 → ℂ := fun _ _ => 2\n86\t\n87\t/-- The witness carrier `D = sigma_x + sigma_z` and its blocks satisfy the\n88\tWeitzenboeck identity `4 D² = Q_A + Q_C + 4 Q_T` (all hypotheses of\n89\t`carrier_square_assembly` discharged for the single-edge `2x2` model). -/\n90\ttheorem witness_assembly :\n91\t    (4 : ℂ) • (solderedNC gammaW nablaW + GammaW * phiW) ^ 2\n92\t      = (∑ e, ∑ f, gW e f • (nablaW e * nablaW f + nablaW f * nablaW e))\n93\t        + (∑ e, ∑ f, (gammaW e * gammaW f - gammaW f * gammaW e)\n94\t            * (nablaW e * nablaW f - nablaW f * nablaW e))\n95\t        + (4 : ℂ) • phiW ^ 2 := by\n96\t  apply carrier_square_assembly (R := ℂ) gammaW nablaW GammaW phiW gW\n97\t  · intro e f\n98\t    fin_cases e; fin_cases f\n99\t    ext i j; fin_cases i <;> fin_cases j <;>\n100\t      norm_num [gammaW, gW, Matrix.mul_apply, Fin.sum_univ_two,\n101\t        Algebra.algebraMap_eq_smul_one, Matrix.one_apply, Matrix.smul_apply]\n102\t  · intro e f; fin_cases e; fin_cases f; simp [gammaW, nablaW]\n103\t  · ext i j; fin_cases i <;> fin_cases j <;>\n104\t      norm_num [GammaW, Matrix.mul_apply, Fin.sum_univ_two, Matrix.one_apply]\n105\t  · intro e; fin_cases e\n106\t    ext i j; fin_cases i <;> fin_cases j <;>\n107\t      norm_num [GammaW, gammaW, Matrix.mul_apply, Fin.sum_univ_two,\n108\t        Matrix.neg_apply]\n109\t  · intro e; fin_cases e; simp [GammaW, nablaW]\n110\t  · intro e; fin_cases e; simp [phiW, gammaW]\n111\t  · simp [GammaW, phiW]\n112\t  · intro e; fin_cases e; simp [nablaW, phiW]\n113\t\n114\t/-- The witness closure share is exactly `0` (single edge -> no closure). -/\n115\ttheorem witness_QC_zero :\n116\t    (∑ e, ∑ f, (gammaW e * gammaW f - gammaW f * gammaW e)\n117\t        * (nablaW e * nablaW f - nablaW f * nablaW e))\n118\t      = (0 : Matrix (Fin 2) (Fin 2) ℂ) := by\n119\t  simp\n120\t\n121\t/-- **The witness mass budget.** With `ev = trace`, the aperture/closure/\n122\tturn shares of the single-edge `2x2` carrier are `1/2, 0, 1/2` and sum to\n123\tone - a non-vacuous instance of `signed_budget_sum_one`. -/\n124\ttheorem witness_budget_sum_one :\n125\t    (Matrix.traceLinearMap (Fin 2) ℂ ℂ)\n126\t          (∑ e, ∑ f, gW e f • (nablaW e * nablaW f + nablaW f * nablaW e))\n127\t        / (4 * (Matrix.traceLinearMap (Fin 2) ℂ ℂ)\n128\t            ((solderedNC gammaW nablaW + GammaW * phiW)\n129\t              * (solderedNC gammaW nablaW + GammaW * phiW)))\n130\t      + (Matrix.traceLinearMap (Fin 2) ℂ ℂ)\n131\t          (∑ e, ∑ f, (gammaW e * gammaW f - gammaW f * gammaW e)\n132\t            * (nablaW e * nablaW f - nablaW f * nablaW e))\n133\t        / (4 * (Matrix.traceLinearMap (Fin 2) ℂ ℂ)\n134\t            ((solderedNC gammaW nablaW + GammaW * phiW)\n135\t              * (solderedNC gammaW nablaW + GammaW * phiW)))\n136\t      + (4 * (Matrix.traceLinearMap (Fin 2) ℂ ℂ) (phiW ^ 2))\n137\t        / (4 * (Matrix.traceLinearMap (Fin 2) ℂ ℂ)\n138\t            ((solderedNC gammaW nablaW + GammaW * phiW)\n139\t              * (solderedNC gammaW nablaW + GammaW * phiW))) = 1 := by\n140\t  have hDD : ((solderedNC gammaW nablaW + GammaW * phiW)\n141\t      * (solderedNC gammaW nablaW + GammaW * phiW))\n142\t      = (2 : ℂ) • (1 : Matrix (Fin 2) (Fin 2) ℂ) := by\n143\t    ext i j; fin_cases i <;> fin_cases j <;>\n144\t      norm_num [solderedNC, gammaW, nablaW, GammaW, phiW,\n145\t        Matrix.mul_apply, Fin.sum_univ_two, Matrix.one_apply,\n146\t        Matrix.smul_apply]\n147\t  have hM : (Matrix.traceLinearMap (Fin 2) ℂ ℂ)\n148\t      ((solderedNC gammaW nablaW + GammaW * phiW)\n149\t        * (solderedNC gammaW nablaW + GammaW * phiW)) ≠ 0 := by\n150\t    rw [hDD]; norm_num [Matrix.traceLinearMap, Matrix.trace_fin_two,\n151\t      Matrix.one_apply, Matrix.smul_apply]\n152\t  have hsq : (solderedNC gammaW nablaW + GammaW * phiW) ^ 2\n153\t      = (solderedNC gammaW nablaW + GammaW * phiW)\n154\t        * (solderedNC gammaW nablaW + GammaW * phiW) := sq _\n155\t  have := signed_budget_sum_one (Matrix.traceLinearMap (Fin 2) ℂ ℂ)\n156\t    (solderedNC gammaW nablaW + GammaW * phiW)\n157\t    (∑ e, ∑ f, gW e f • (nablaW e * nablaW f + nablaW f * nablaW e))\n158\t    (∑ e, ∑ f, (gammaW e * gammaW f - gammaW f * gammaW e)\n159\t      * (nablaW e * nablaW f - nablaW f * nablaW e))\n160\t    (phiW ^ 2) (by rw [← hsq]; no `s o r r y`). The overnight all-mass run's K4 -\nthe flagship S6 deliverable, in its Amendment-A3 SIGNED form: the finite\nmass budget of a state splits EXACTLY into aperture / closure / turn\nshares that sum to one, with the shares SIGNED (not asserted positive\nuntil the physical-sector positivity crux closes).\n\n## The two parts\n\n* `signed_budget_sum_one` (abstract): given the guard-pinned Weitzenboeck\n  identity `4 . D² = Q_A + Q_C + 4 . Q_T` and ANY linear functional `ev`\n  (the expectation `<psi, . psi>`) with `ev (D²) != 0`, the shares\n  `b_A = ev Q_A / M²`, `b_C = ev Q_C / M²`, `b_T = 4 ev Q_T / M²`\n  (`M² = 4 ev(D²)`) satisfy `b_A + b_C + b_T = 1`.\n* `witness_budget_*` (vacuity guard): a concrete `2x2` single-edge\n  carrier - `gamma = sigma_x`, `Gamma = sigma_z`, `nabla = 1`, `phi = 1`,\n  `g = 2` - discharges every hypothesis of `carrier_square_assembly`, so\n  the identity holds by construction; with `ev = trace` the closure share\n  is exactly `0` (one edge -> no closure) and the budget is\n  `b_A = 1/2, b_C = 0, b_T = 1/2` - a non-vacuous witness.\n\nThis is the 3-slot `D²` budget (matching the pinned assembly); the 4-slot\n`D^#D` budget with the soldering-gradient share `E` awaits the `D^#D`\nassembly. No positivity is claimed: the shares are signed (Amendment A3);\n`b_C` is the CHROMOMAGNETIC (hyperfine) share, NOT a gluon-energy share\n(Amendment B wording rail).\n\n## Claim boundary\n\n`b_A + b_C + b_T = 1` is proved (abstract + witness). The physical reading\n(non-turn dominance, hadron budget) is prose. The witness is Euclidean\n(shares happen to be positive); the SIGNED generality is the theorem's,\nrealized once an indefinite `ev` / larger carrier is used. No continuum,\nno hadron-mass claim.\n\n## Provenance\n\nS6 shape from the QCD roadmap (Amendment A3, SevenChallenges finding 7) -\n[comp]; built on the guard-pinned `carrier_square_assembly`\n(`CarrierSquareAssembly.lean`) - [orig].\n-/\n\nimport PhysicsSM.Draft.NullEdge.Carrier.CarrierSquareAssembly\n\nnamespace PhysicsSM.Draft.NullEdge.Carrier.MassBudget\n\nopen Matrix\n\n/-! ## The abstract signed budget -/\n\n/-- **Signed mass-budget decomposition of unity.** From the Weitzenboeck\nidentity `4 . D² = Q_A + Q_C + 4 . Q_T` and a linear expectation `ev` with\n`ev (D * D) != 0`, the aperture/closure/turn shares of `M² = 4 ev(D²)` sum\nto one. Shares are signed - no positivity assumed. -/\ntheorem signed_budget_sum_one {B : Type*} [Ring B] [Algebra ℂ B]\n    (ev : B →ₗ[ℂ] ℂ) (D QA QC QT : B)\n    (hid : (4 : ℂ) • (D * D) = QA + QC + (4 : ℂ) • QT)\n    (hM : ev (D * D) ≠ 0) :\n    ev QA / (4 * ev (D * D)) + ev QC / (4 * ev (D * D))\n        + (4 * ev QT) / (4 * ev (D * D)) = 1 := by\n  have hev : (4 : ℂ) * ev (D * D) = ev QA + ev QC + 4 * ev QT := by\n    have h := congrArg ev hid\n    rw [map_smul, map_add, map_add, map_smul, smul_eq_mul, smul_eq_mul] at h\n    exact h\n  have hs : (4 : ℂ) * ev (D * D) ≠ 0 := mul_ne_zero (by norm_num) hM\n  rw [← add_div, ← add_div, ← hev, div_self hs]\n\n/-! ## A concrete non-vacuous witness: the 2x2 single-edge carrier -/\n\n/-- Single Clifford generator `sigma_x`. -/\ndef gammaW : Fin 1 → Matrix (Fin 2) (Fin 2) ℂ := fun _ => !![0, 1; 1, 0]\n\n/-- Constant unit transport `nabla = 1`. -/\ndef nablaW : Fin 1 → Matrix (Fin 2) (Fin 2) ℂ := fun _ => 1\n\n/-- Chirality grading `sigma_z`. -/\ndef GammaW : Matrix (Fin 2) (Fin 2) ℂ := !![1, 0; 0, -1]\n\n/-- Unit turn field `phi = 1`. -/\ndef phiW : Matrix (Fin 2) (Fin 2) ℂ := 1\n\n/-- Edge metric `g = 2` (from `{sigma_x, sigma_x} = 2`). -/\ndef gW : Fin 1 → Fin 1 → ℂ := fun _ _ => 2\n\n/-- The witness carrier `D = sigma_x + sigma_z` and its blocks satisfy the\nWeitzenboeck identity `4 D² = Q_A + Q_C + 4 Q_T` (all hypotheses of\n`carrier_square_assembly` discharged for the single-edge `2x2` model). -/\ntheorem witness_assembly :\n    (4 : ℂ) • (solderedNC gammaW nablaW + GammaW * phiW) ^ 2\n      = (∑ e, ∑ f, gW e f • (nablaW e * nablaW f + nablaW f * nablaW e))\n        + (∑ e, ∑ f, (gammaW e * gammaW f - gammaW f * gammaW e)\n            * (nablaW e * nablaW f - nablaW f * nablaW e))\n        + (4 : ℂ) • phiW ^ 2 := by\n  apply carrier_square_assembly (R := ℂ) gammaW nablaW GammaW phiW gW\n  · intro e f\n    fin_cases e; fin_cases f\n    ext i j; fin_cases i <;> fin_cases j <;>\n      norm_num [gammaW, gW, Matrix.mul_apply, Fin.sum_univ_two,\n        Algebra.algebraMap_eq_smul_one, Matrix.one_apply, Matrix.smul_apply]\n  · intro e f; fin_cases e; fin_cases f; simp [gammaW, nablaW]\n  · ext i j; fin_cases i <;> fin_cases j <;>\n      norm_num [GammaW, Matrix.mul_apply, Fin.sum_univ_two, Matrix.one_apply]\n  · intro e; fin_cases e\n    ext i j; fin_cases i <;> fin_cases j <;>\n      norm_num [GammaW, gammaW, Matrix.mul_apply, Fin.sum_univ_two,\n        Matrix.neg_apply]\n  · intro e; fin_cases e; simp [GammaW, nablaW]\n  · intro e; fin_cases e; simp [phiW, gammaW]\n  · simp [GammaW, phiW]\n  · intro e; fin_cases e; simp [nablaW, phiW]\n\n/-- The witness closure share is exactly `0` (single edge -> no closure). -/\ntheorem witness_QC_zero :\n    (∑ e, ∑ f, (gammaW e * gammaW f - gammaW f * gammaW e)\n        * (nablaW e * nablaW f - nablaW f * nablaW e))\n      = (0 : Matrix (Fin 2) (Fin 2) ℂ) := by\n  simp\n\n/-- **The witness mass budget.** With `ev = trace`, the aperture/closure/\nturn shares of the single-edge `2x2` carrier are `1/2, 0, 1/2` and sum to\none - a non-vacuous instance of `signed_budget_sum_one`. -/\ntheorem witness_budget_sum_one :\n    (Matrix.traceLinearMap (Fin 2) ℂ ℂ)\n          (∑ e, ∑ f, gW e f • (nablaW e * nablaW f + nablaW f * nablaW e))\n        / (4 * (Matrix.traceLinearMap (Fin 2) ℂ ℂ)\n            ((solderedNC gammaW nablaW + GammaW * phiW)\n              * (solderedNC gammaW nablaW + GammaW * phiW)))\n      + (Matrix.traceLinearMap (Fin 2) ℂ ℂ)\n          (∑ e, ∑ f, (gammaW e * gammaW f - gammaW f * gammaW e)\n            * (nablaW e * nablaW f - nablaW f * nablaW e))\n        / (4 * (Matrix.traceLinearMap (Fin 2) ℂ ℂ)\n            ((solderedNC gammaW nablaW + GammaW * phiW)\n              * (solderedNC gammaW nablaW + GammaW * phiW)))\n      + (4 * (Matrix.traceLinearMap (Fin 2) ℂ ℂ) (phiW ^ 2))\n        / (4 * (Matrix.traceLinearMap (Fin 2) ℂ ℂ)\n            ((solderedNC gammaW nablaW + GammaW * phiW)\n              * (solderedNC gammaW nablaW + GammaW * phiW))) = 1 := by\n  have hDD : ((solderedNC gammaW nablaW + GammaW * phiW)\n      * (solderedNC gammaW nablaW + GammaW * phiW))\n      = (2 : ℂ) • (1 : Matrix (Fin 2) (Fin 2) ℂ) := by\n    ext i j; fin_cases i <;> fin_cases j <;>\n      norm_num [solderedNC, gammaW, nablaW, GammaW, phiW,\n        Matrix.mul_apply, Fin.sum_univ_two, Matrix.one_apply,\n        Matrix.smul_apply]\n  have hM : (Matrix.traceLinearMap (Fin 2) ℂ ℂ)\n      ((solderedNC gammaW nablaW + GammaW * phiW)\n        * (solderedNC gammaW nablaW + GammaW * phiW)) ≠ 0 := by\n    rw [hDD]; norm_num [Matrix.traceLinearMap, Matrix.trace_fin_two,\n      Matrix.one_apply, Matrix.smul_apply]\n  have hsq : (solderedNC gammaW nablaW + GammaW * phiW) ^ 2\n      = (solderedNC gammaW nablaW + GammaW * phiW)\n        * (solderedNC gammaW nablaW + GammaW * phiW) := sq _\n  have := signed_budget_sum_one (Matrix.traceLinearMap (Fin 2) ℂ ℂ)\n    (solderedNC gammaW nablaW + GammaW * phiW)\n    (∑ e, ∑ f, gW e f • (nablaW e * nablaW f + nablaW f * nablaW e))\n    (∑ e, ∑ f, (gammaW e * gammaW f - gammaW f * gammaW e)\n      * (nablaW e * nablaW f - nablaW f * nablaW e))\n    (phiW ^ 2) (by rw [← hsq]; exact witness_assembly) hM\n  exact this\n\nend PhysicsSM.Draft.NullEdge.Carrier.MassBudget\n","numLines":164,"startLine":1,"totalLines":164}}}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_019b2kRgK3wzfbrobEAQJ5z2","type":"tool_result","content":"import Mathlib\n\n/-!\n# NullEdge.Carrier.ColorCommutantScalar — the [H2] color-commutant constraint\n\nThis module realizes **Fable-5's reframe of `OctonionMassCoupling`** (see\n`PhysicsSM.Algebra.Furey.OctonionMassCoupling` and\n`AgentTasks/overnight-mass-run-2026-07-06/FABLE_STEER.md` §5.4 / [H2]).\n\n## The [H2] audit, made precise\n\n`OctonionMassCoupling` proved that a **non-degenerate diagonal grading**\n`diag(m0, m1, m2)` on the color triplet **fails to commute** with the octonionic\n`su(3)` color action. Read as physics that result is a **CONSTRAINT** (a small\nno-go), not a mass mechanism: color is an *exact* symmetry, so a genuine physical\nmass/Yukawa operator must lie in the **commutant** of the color action. Hence a\nnon-degenerate color-non-singlet grading is simply **not a physical (color-exact)\nmass** on the triplet.\n\nThis file proves the **positive companion** to that no-go. On a *single*\nirreducible fundamental triplet `ℂ³` the color commutant is exactly the\n**scalars**:\n\n* `color_commutant_eq_scalars` (**headline**, Schur's lemma made concrete): a\n  matrix `Mx` commutes with every color generator **iff** `Mx = c • 1` for some\n  `c : ℂ`.\n* `scalar_mass_is_color_exact` (easy direction, stated separately): every scalar\n  `c • 1` commutes with every color generator — i.e. scalars are color-exact.\n* `nonscalar_mass_not_color_exact` (**constraint corollary**, the honest reading\n  of `mass_grading_not_central`): a diagonal mass `diagonal ![m0, m1, m2]`\n  commutes with every color generator **iff** it is color-blind\n  (`m0 = m1 ∧ m1 = m2`). In particular `diagonal ![1, 2, 3]` does **not** commute\n  with the color action, so it is not a color-exact mass operator.\n\nSo the only color-exact mass on one triplet is a color-blind scalar. This is the\nprecise content of the [H2] constraint on the single-irreducible-triplet base\ncase.\n\n## Generators\n\nWe work with `M := Matrix (Fin 3) (Fin 3) ℂ` acting on the color triplet `ℂ³`,\nand use the **standard `su(3)` fundamental generators**, reconstructed to match\nthe action table of `PhysicsSM.Algebra.Furey.OctonionMassCoupling`: the two\nCartan generators `H23m`, `H13m` and the six ladders `T12m, T21m, T13m, T31m,\nT23m, T32m`. Their span is the fundamental `su(3)` action on `ℂ³`; the six\nladders alone realize all six off-diagonal elementary matrices `E_{i,j}`\n(`i ≠ j`), which is what forces the commutant down to the scalars (this is Schur's\nlemma for the irreducible fundamental).\n\n## Scope / honesty (draft-trust, finite linear algebra)\n\nThe place where genuine flavor / generation / Yukawa structure can live is the\ncommutant on the **FULL reducible internal space** (the multiplicity spaces, per\nSchur `⨁ End(mult space)`). That is **OPEN / out of scope** here — **not**\ncomputed in this file. This module is only the single-irreducible-triplet base\ncase: finite linear algebra, draft-trust.\n\n## Axiom footprint\n\nThe final theorems use only Lean/Mathlib standard axioms\n====\nimport Mathlib\n\n/-!\n# Finite second-quantization identities\n\nThis module records small, kernel-checked finite shadows of the Q08\nsecond-quantization program.  The flagship exterior-algebra quotient theorem\nand the general `dGamma` square identity are intentionally left to the active\nAristotle lanes; here we pin a minimal two-mode diagonal witness:\n\n`dGamma(D)^2 = dGamma(D^2) + 2 dGamma_2(Lambda^2 D)`.\n\nFor two fermion modes and a diagonal one-particle operator with entries `d 0`\nand `d 1`, the second-quantized diagonal action on an occupation state is the\nsum of occupied one-particle eigenvalues.  Squaring it produces the one-body\nsquare plus exactly the two-body cross term when both modes are occupied.\n\nProvenance: Q08 Fable answer, Section 4 / ladder `L-Q8-3`, clean-room finite\nformalization of the diagonal two-mode sanity check.\n-/\n\nnamespace PhysicsSM.Draft.NullEdge.Carrier.FockSecondQuantization\n\n/-- Two fermion modes, represented by their occupation bits. -/\nabbrev Fock2State := Fin 2 -> Bool\n\n/-- Diagonal second quantization on the two-mode occupation basis:\nsum the eigenvalues of the occupied modes. -/\ndef dGammaDiag {R : Type*} [Zero R] [Add R] (d : Fin 2 -> R) (occ : Fock2State) : R :=\n  (if occ 0 then d 0 else 0) + (if occ 1 then d 1 else 0)\n\n/-- The two-body diagonal contribution, present exactly on the doubly occupied state. -/\ndef dGammaPairDiag {R : Type*} [Zero R] [Mul R] (d : Fin 2 -> R) (occ : Fock2State) :\n    R :=\n  if occ 0 && occ 1 then d 0 * d 1 else 0\n\n/-- Two-mode diagonal `dGamma` square identity.\n\nThis is the finite occupation-basis witness for the Q08 interaction slogan:\nsquaring the second-quantized one-particle operator gives the second\nquantization of the square plus the canonical two-body cross term. -/\ntheorem dGammaDiag_square_two_mode {R : Type*} [CommSemiring R] (d : Fin 2 -> R)\n    (occ : Fock2State) :\n    dGammaDiag d occ ^ 2 =\n      dGammaDiag (fun i => d i ^ 2) occ + 2 * dGammaPairDiag d occ := by\n  by_cases h0 : occ 0\n  · by_cases h1 : occ 1\n    · simp [dGammaDiag, dGammaPairDiag, h0, h1]\n      ring\n    · simp [dGammaDiag, dGammaPairDiag, h0, (see\n`PhysicsSM.Algebra.Furey.OctonionMassCoupling` and\n`AgentTasks/overnight-mass-run-2026-07-06/FABLE_STEER.md` §5.4 / [H2]).\n\n## The [H2] audit, made precise\n\n`OctonionMassCoupling` proved that a **non-degenerate diagonal grading**\n`diag(m0, m1, m2)` on the color triplet **fails to commute** with the octonionic\n`su(3)` color action. Read as physics that result is a **CONSTRAINT** (a small\nno-go), not a mass mechanism: color is an *exact* symmetry, so a genuine physical\nmass/Yukawa operator must lie in the **commutant** of the color action. Hence a\nnon-degenerate color-non-singlet grading is simply **not a physical (color-exact)\nmass** on the triplet.\n\nThis file proves the **positive companion** to that no-go. On a *single*\nirreducible fundamental triplet `ℂ³` the color commutant is exactly the\n**scalars**:\n\n* `color_commutant_eq_scalars` (**headline**, Schur's lemma made concrete): a\n  matrix `Mx` commutes with every color generator **iff** `Mx = c • 1` for some\n  `c : ℂ`.\n* `scalar_mass_is_color_exact` (easy direction, stated separately): every scalar\n  `c • 1` commutes with every color generator — i.e. scalars are color-exact.\n* `nonscalar_mass_not_color_exact` (**constraint corollary**, the honest reading\n  of `mass_grading_not_central`): a diagonal mass `diagonal ![m0, m1, m2]`\n  commutes with every color generator **iff** it is color-blind\n  (`m0 = m1 ∧ m1 = m2`). In particular `diagonal ![1, 2, 3]` does **not** commute\n  with the color action, so it is not a color-exact mass operator.\n\nSo the only color-exact mass on one triplet is a color-blind scalar.
